# Changelog

This changelog documents all the different updates that occur for this framework.

## [2.3.0] - unreleased

The RAG Builder becomes part of the Prompt Builder: one report, one job definition and one Options pane for prompt templates, decision templates and RAG setups, with a switch for each of decision templates, prompt optimization and RAG. A new option lets an administrator switch TLS verification off for manifested models where a destination cannot verify. The release also fixes what a verification of 2.2.0 found, first of all that a manifested decision template could not be scored. Three fixes of the RAG runtime are part of it - the run history that is recorded again, a saved ledger that survives a restart of the CAS server, and a job generated from a flow that ends as failed when a step fails - and a failed run of the ingestion job of a setup now ends as a failed job as well and hands its run lock back, so that it can be retried at once - both measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, where the RAG runtime of this release was run once more; the entries say what was measured there and what was not run. The log parser reads the calls of every kind of model and survives an hour without traffic, and the log collection of the Administration Guide keeps provider keys and cookies out of the collected file. The log table says of every request what kind it is and whether it failed, `Get-All-Prompts.sas` pulls the experiments of prompt templates and decision templates and the RAG setups with their SAS Model Manager ids, and a new program links every log row to the model that answered and to what sent the request. These three ran as one chain on the test system on 2026-10-11 and twice on 2026-10-12, each time as they were then; each run led to changes, and the entries say what was measured and which part is not run in SAS. The ingestion job of a RAG setup publishes its run history to CAS, as the step *RAG - Load Vector Store* does - measured on 2026-10-12. The content folder `/SAS Agentic AI Accelerator` ships as one transfer package that is built from a list and compared with the checkout, with a first page, *Start Here*, that checks an installation, and the upgrade is one command. The commands and the programs for this ran in a test content root on the test system on 2026-10-12, as they were then. What that pass found changed them - above all the import, which now hands only the reports to the SAS Viya transfer service and writes every file, custom step and job definition in place under the id it has on the site -, and what was changed after the pass has not run against SAS Viya; the entries say which is which. The package file itself is exported after this commit.

### Upgrading from 2.2.x

One transfer package, `SAS-Viya-Integrations/SAS-Agentic-AI-Accelerator.json`, now carries everything under `/SAS Agentic AI Accelerator`: the Prompt Builder with the RAG Builder in it, the optimization job, the RAG runtime, the logging programs with their two custom steps and the usage report, the files the tables of the reports are loaded from, and the page *Start Here*. The report named `RAG Builder` is retired. Steps 1 to 4 are for every site; of the four blocks after them a site takes the ones for what it uses, and each step names the section of the guides that has the details and what was measured. **The package file of this release is not in the repository yet**: it is exported from the content folder after this commit, and until then `mdb builders-import` says that the checkout holds no package and ends with exit code 2 (see *The content folder ships as one transfer package* under *Added*).

**Everyone.** The import brings the content folder of this release - the two reports through the SAS Viya transfer service, every file, custom step and job definition in place, under the id it has on your site - and the command keeps the options an administrator set on the Prompt Builder report.

1. Update the checkout to 2.3.0 and reinstall the CLI: `pip install -e "Model-Definition-Builder/cli[viya]"`.
2. `mdb builders-import`, then reload the Prompt Builder report: the command keeps the options of the report, and of the report `RAG Builder` while it exists - [First install and upgrade](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Setup-Additional-UIs#first-install-and-upgrade). A site that had the report `RAG Builder` and does not use RAG reads [The retired RAG Builder report](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Setup-Additional-UIs#the-retired-rag-builder-report) first.
3. Review `sas-viya-cli-content-commands.txt`, which step 2 wrote, and run its two commands: the package carries no authorization rule, and they give the *LLM Consumers* group Read on the content folder. See [Access to the content folder](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Setup-Additional-UIs#access-to-the-content-folder).
4. Open the page [Start Here](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Setup-Additional-UIs#start-here) - `/SASJobExecution/?_program=/SAS Agentic AI Accelerator/Start Here` - and go through the lines of its installation check that say `missing`. Where the page says that every request was refused, add `&_contextName=<a compute context that runs as you>` to its address.
5. Only for sites that publish Python models to SAS Micro Analytic Service: read [Publishing to SAS Micro Analytic Service: the certificate bundle](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Deployment-of-Decisions#mas-certificate-bundle) - without the CA bundle in the Python runner such a model returns `CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED`.

**Prompt building.** Nothing to do: the import of step 2 brings the UI and the optimization job with the program of this release.

**Decision models.** Only for sites that manifested a decision template with 2.2.0.

6. Manifest each of those decision templates again (open it in the Prompt Builder, go to *Finalize*, manifest): the score code 2.2.0 wrote is not valid Python (see *Fixed*). A question whose name the designer now refuses has to be renamed first. See [Decision templates](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/User-Guide/Prompt-Builder#decision-templates).

**RAG.** Only for sites that build RAG setups. The import of step 2 brings the RAG runtime of this release into the folder `RAG` of the content folder.

7. Only where the option **RAG: content root** names another folder than `/SAS Agentic AI Accelerator/RAG`: deploy the runtime into that folder as well, from the checkout, with `mdb content-deploy --only RAG --to "<your RAG content root>"`. See [What gets deployed](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/RAG-Ingestion-and-Retrieval#what-gets-deployed).
8. In the Prompt Builder, open every RAG setup, click **Manifest setup** and run its ingestion once: the job definition of a setup holds a copy of the ingestion program, and that run publishes the run history of the setup to CAS. Then publish the decisions that use its retrieval model again. For a setup registered from a flow, run the step *RAG - Register Setup* again: [After an upgrade: manifest every setup once](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/RAG-Ingestion-and-Retrieval#after-an-upgrade).
9. Once the Prompt Builder report shows your setups, delete the report `RAG Builder` and the job definition `RAG-Builder-UI` next to it: [The retired RAG Builder report](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Setup-Additional-UIs#the-retired-rag-builder-report).
10. Only where the user an ingestion connects to the vector store with is not the owner of the table `rag_runs`: its owner runs an ingestion, or a DBA runs `ALTER TABLE rag_runs ADD COLUMN docs_excluded integer DEFAULT 0` once. See [Run history](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/RAG-Ingestion-and-Retrieval#run-history).
11. Only for your own builds and hand-written URLs: `npm run build:rag` is gone, and a URL that opens the RAG Builder names the Prompt Builder job with `enableRag=true` and `ragComputeContext` (see *Removed*): [Serving the RAG Builder](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/RAG-Ingestion-and-Retrieval#serving-the-rag-builder).

**Logging.** Only for sites that collect the container logs. The import of step 2 brings the parser, the pull of what the Prompt Builder keeps in SAS Model Manager, the new link program, the two custom steps and the usage report; an existing `LLM_LOGS` needs no rebuild, and a job or a schedule that runs one of the three programs has to expect a run that is refused to end as failed: [Reading the Job Log](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Logging-&-Monitoring#reading-the-job-log).

12. Replace your log collection script with the one in [Simplified Logging via a Script](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Logging-&-Monitoring#simplified-logging-via-a-script) (it needs GNU `sed`): it runs where `kubectl` runs and is in no package.
13. Delete the log files collected so far, with their copies and backups, and rotate the provider keys that were in use while they were collected: the files can hold keys and cookies in clear text.
14. Only where a flow uses *LLM - Log Parser* or *LLM - Get All Prompts* from another folder than `Logging and Monitoring` - the starter package of earlier releases had the parser in `Custom Steps` -, which the import of step 2 does not touch: update that step in place from the checkout with `mdb content-deploy --only "Logging and Monitoring/LLM - Log Parser.step" --to "<path or id of your step>"` - [One part, somewhere else](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Model-Definition-Builder#one-part-somewhere-else).
15. Only where you run the programs and not the custom steps: put your settings into a program of your own that sets them and includes the shipped file - the import replaced the files, and from this release on a setting that is set before a program runs wins: [Settings that stay yours](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Logging-&-Monitoring#settings-that-stay-yours).
16. Only for sites that want every log row linked to its model: run the programs in the order collect, parse, pull, link - [Linking the Log to the Models](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Logging-&-Monitoring#settings-of-the-link) - and, after step 8, the pull again and the link with `_lnk_redo_all = 1`, so that the ingestion runs the setups published are found.

A first install is two commands, `mdb setup` and then `mdb builders-import`, and then the page *Start Here*. The Prompt Builder opens with RAG switched off, until **Enable RAG setups** is ticked.

### Added

- **A RAG setup is the third kind on the Setup card of the Prompt Builder.** *What are you building?* now offers up to three kinds, each explained in a sentence: a prompt template for LLMs, a decision template for decision models, and a **RAG setup for a document collection**. Choosing the RAG setup shows the RAG Builder's own four steps - *Setup*, *Documents & chunks*, *Store & output*, *Run & test* - with the same question at the top of its Setup card; choosing a kind of template brings the prompt steps back. Nothing is lost on the way: both sides stay loaded, each keeps what is selected, typed and running, and an ingestion or an experiment keeps reporting while the other side is shown. The RAG side is loaded the first time it is chosen (the card shows that it is loading); if that fails you stay where you were with the reason under the cards, and choosing the card again retries. A change between the two template kinds is refused with a message while an experiment or an optimization is running. A kind is offered only where it is switched on and configured, and with one kind left the question is not shown at all.
- **Three switches in the Options pane decide what the Prompt Builder offers.** **Enable decision templates** (`enableDecision`, ticked by default), **Enable prompt optimization (DSPy)** (`enableOptimization`, not ticked by default, as before) and **Enable RAG setups** (`enableRag`, not ticked by default), each a checkbox. A feature that is switched off shows nothing and asks SAS Viya for nothing: without decision templates there is no decision kind, no Evaluate step, no lookup of the *Decision Model Project* and no call to a decision model, and existing decision projects and templates are not listed (they stay untouched in SAS Model Manager); without RAG nothing of the RAG Builder is loaded, and with it enabled nothing is requested for it until the kind is chosen. The options that belong to a switch appear directly below it only while it is ticked, with labels that start with `Optimization:` and `RAG:`. A report stores a switch as the word `true` or `false`, and Visual Analytics writes that word into the URL of the object. A URL written by hand may also say `1` / `yes` / `on` and `0` / `no` / `off`; anything else, and a missing or blank value, is the default.
- **The Prompt Builder runs with RAG alone, and says what prompt and decision templates still need and what RAG setups still need.** A site without an LLM project gets the RAG Builder by itself once RAG is enabled and the repository ID is set. Where nothing usable is configured, *Configuration required* names what prompt and decision templates still need and what RAG setups still need - completing either line is enough for the page to load. With the credential domain set to `none`, which RAG cannot work with, the RAG card is shown disabled with that reason instead of failing later.
- **An option decides whether models manifested in the Prompt Builder verify TLS certificates.** **Verify TLS certificates in manifested models** (`sslVerify`) is a checkbox in the Options pane, directly after the credential domain, and ticked by default. Its state is written into the score code of every model manifested from then on that makes an HTTPS call itself: a prompt template with the included LLM call, a decision template, and the retrieval model of a RAG setup. It is a default written at manifest time - a model that is already manifested keeps what it was manifested with until it is manifested again - and it changes nothing the page does in the browser, nothing in the ingestion job, and nothing in a prompt manifested for the *Call LLM* node, where the node makes the call. With the option ticked the generated code behaves as before, and a report saved before the option existed shows it ticked. The reason for the option, measured on one deployment: SAS Micro Analytic Service runs Python there in a runner container that does not mount the CA bundle of the deployment, so a published decision whose model calls the model container itself returns `CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED ... self-signed certificate in certificate chain` in every row, while the same decision works in the in-app test of SAS Intelligent Decisioning. With its decision template manifested while the option was not ticked, the same decision returned answers on that destination. Mounting the bundle file into that container - the one file, not the volume it lies in, which also holds the private key directory of the pod - keeps verification on and is the fix for production; switching verification off gives up the check that the answer comes from the real model container and is for a test system on a closed cluster network. The new section *Publishing to SAS Micro Analytic Service: the certificate bundle* on the *Deployment of Decisions* page of the Administration Guide has the symptom, how to recognise the case, and the patch in both forms. The mount was applied on that deployment on 2026-10-11, as the one-off patch of that section: the new pod became ready, the runner then listed `trustedcerts.pem` under `/security` and nothing else, and the same decision, with its decision template manifested with verification on, returned four answers in a publishing validation of four rows where it had returned the error before. A second decision was published there the same day, with three model nodes that were all manifested in the Prompt Builder - the retrieval model of a RAG setup, a prompt template with the included LLM call and a decision template - and with verification on: all three nodes ran, the publishing validation ended on three rows with HTTP status 201 and an empty error column, and the prompt template and the decision template answered. The retrieval model answered `retrieval failed: RuntimeError: no vector-store credentials resolved` with no chunk, there and in the test of SAS Intelligent Decisioning: neither runtime had `PGVECTOR_RAG_USER` and `PGVECTOR_RAG_PW` (*The retrieval model in a decision* on the *RAG Ingestion and Retrieval* page names the variables); what was measured is that status text, not which requests the model made before it. With the user and the password of the vector store passed per call in `options`, the retrieval model then returned chunks in that decision on the same destination, with verification on in the score code of all three models - in single calls, and in a publishing validation only when its rows finished within the 30 seconds of the service (see *The Administration Guide says how to recognise an execution …* below). The kustomize form of the patch, and the call of a retrieval model to the credentials service from SAS Micro Analytic Service - which it does not make when the credentials come with the call or from the environment - were not measured. `mdb options-save` and `mdb options-restore` carry the option like any other; an options file saved with 2.2.x does not name it, so the imported report keeps its default, and on a report that would ship it switched off `mdb package-export` ends with exit code 1 and writes nothing.
- **A model manifested in the Prompt Builder without TLS verification is marked, and the step says so before the click.** Such a model carries the tag `TLS-Verification-Off` in SAS Model Manager, so the models that do not verify can be listed with a filter on that tag; manifesting it again with the option ticked removes the tag, on all three kinds. While the option is not ticked, the step shows a warning before the click: *Finalize* for a decision template, *Finalize* for a prompt template only while **Include the LLM call in the manifested model** is ticked, and *Manifest setup* for a RAG setup. The warning names the option and the environment variable that turns verification back on for a destination: `LLMCONTAINERSSLVERIFY=true`, and for the retrieval model `RAGEMBED_SSLVERIFY=true` and `RAGSTORE_SSLVERIFY=true`. The tag follows the score code the model holds: when the tags of a model could not be updated, the manifest says so in both directions - the tag could not be written to a model that does not verify, or could not be taken away from one that does. The one case the page cannot decide, a score code whose upload gets no answer, is described under *Fixed*. In the score code the setting is one line: `sslVerifyDefault = True` or `sslVerifyDefault = False` in a prompt or decision template, `SSL_VERIFY = True` or `SSL_VERIFY = False` in the MANIFEST block of a retrieval model. Nothing else in the score code of a prompt or decision template can carry the name of that line: a prompt variable, an output variable or a question named `sslVerifyDefault` is refused in the Prompt Builder, like any other name the score code uses itself.
- **The custom step `RAG - Register Setup` has a checkbox for TLS verification in the model it registers.** The option **Verify TLS certificates in manifested models** reached a retrieval model manifested from the page and no model registered with the step: `rag_core` wrote no `SSL_VERIFY` line, so a model the step registered verified whatever was wanted and never carried the tag. The step (version 1.3) has the checkbox **Verify TLS certificates in the registered retrieval model** in its *Runtime* section, ticked by default. Ticked, the model is registered with `SSL_VERIFY = True`, and a tag `TLS-Verification-Off` from an earlier registration is removed. Not ticked, the model is registered with `SSL_VERIFY = False` and tagged `TLS-Verification-Off`; the log of the step ends with WARNING lines - the model does not verify the certificates of its two HTTPS calls, to the embedding container and to the credentials service, and `RAGEMBED_SSLVERIFY=true` and `RAGSTORE_SSLVERIFY=true` on the publishing destination turn verification back on - and the registration report shows the state in the *Detail* column of the row of the model. The tag is set or removed after the score code is stored, so a registration that fails at the upload leaves it as it was - also when the model could not be read before the upload: the tag is then kept as the listing of the models shows it - and a tag that could not be updated is a WARNING of its own, in both directions. Where the listing does not show the tags of such a model either, nothing says whether it carries the tag, and the step writes it: a tag too many is put right by the next registration that goes through. A run whose score code is then not stored, or not known to be, says in WARNING lines of its own that the model carries a tag this run put there (*the model could not be read before this run updated it …*) - checked with the test suite, not run in SAS: the case needs a SAS Model Manager that does not return a model while its listing shows no tags, a fault of the service that could not be staged on the test system. A run that fails after the score code was stored - the flow named under *Studio Flow to generate the scheduled job from* does not exist, say - leaves a model that holds the new score code, and the log of the failing run says so below its ERROR line and then writes the same WARNING lines: whether the stored model verifies, and whether it carries the tag. An upload of the score code that gets no usable answer (the connection drops, a time-out, the page of an ingress) is reported as what it is: the step says that it is not known whether SAS Model Manager stored the file, that the tag was not updated and may not say what the stored score code does, and that the step has to be run again - the wording of the Builder for the same case; nothing is read back. On a model whose tag the run wrote before the upload, without knowing whether the model carried it, the line says *was not updated after the upload* (see *Fixed*). An upload SAS Model Manager refuses with an answer of its own leaves the model with the score code it had, and with the tag it had unless the run wrote the tag before the upload. `rag_core` writes the line under the same rule as the page, only into a template that has it: with a template deployed before this release and the box not ticked, the step registers a model that still verifies, does not tag it, and warns that verification is still ON and that the RAG content has to be deployed again. A flow that hands the step no value for the checkbox registers with verification on. The checkbox changes the registered model only: the RAG steps and the ingestion job verify the calls they make themselves with the CA bundle of the compute session and have no switch for it, and the TLS of the connection to the vector store stays the SSL mode. The step and `rag_core` reach a site with the transfer package (step 2 of the upgrade). The step was run in SAS Studio on 2026-10-11, on SAS Viya 2026.09, as the last step of a flow that was created without a value for the checkbox: the checkbox shows ticked in the *Runtime* section; run that way the step ended green, logged *TLS certificate verification on* and stored `SSL_VERIFY = True`; not ticked, the node showed a warning badge, the log held the WARNING lines, the score code held `SSL_VERIFY = False` and the model carried `TLS-Verification-Off` beside its four tags; ticked again, the tag was gone and the four tags were kept; with a template without the `SSL_VERIFY` line and the box not ticked, the log held the three WARNING lines about a model that still verifies, and the model was not tagged. Measured later the same day, with the step run as the code SAS Studio generates for a flow and not clicked in SAS Studio: a run that failed after the score code was stored, with the box not ticked and a flow that does not exist, wrote its ERROR line, the WARNING that the model holds the score code, the three lines about a model that does not verify and the line about the tag, in that order, and the model held `SSL_VERIFY = False` and the tag. Not measured: a job generated from a flow that contains the step, a model registered by the step running inside a decision or in SAS Micro Analytic Service, and - checked with the test suite only - the WARNING lines of an upload without a usable answer and of a tag that could not be written or removed. They were not run on 2026-10-11 either: each needs a fault of SAS Model Manager - a model it does not return, an upload it does not answer - and not a state a model of the test can be put in, and the logs of the two runs of the step made that day hold none of these lines.
- **The content folder ships as one transfer package, built from a list.** `SAS-Viya-Integrations/package-manifest.yaml` lists every object of `/SAS Agentic AI Accelerator` that ships: one entry per object with its path below the content root, its kind - folder, file, custom step, job definition, report - and the repository file it is a copy of. It lists 61 objects - 37 files, 18 custom steps, four job definitions and two reports - in the folders `Prompt Builder`, `RAG`, `Logging and Monitoring`, `Data` and `Custom Steps`, with `Start Here` and `release.json` at the root. An object that is not listed is not deployed, not exported and fails the check when it is found in the package. A path with `Showcases` or `Example` in a name, a folder named `generated` and a name that starts with `ZZ_VERIFY` are refused when the list is loaded and again for every object an export would keep: showcase and example content, what the Prompt Builder generates for a site's own setups and test objects never ship. One step of `Custom-Steps` is not listed: *LLM - Get Prompt Experiments*, the step of August 2024 that reads the experiment tracker of one model, where *LLM - Get All Prompts* pulls the experiments. The one file `SAS-Viya-Integrations/SAS-Agentic-AI-Accelerator.json` takes the place of the four packages of earlier releases (see *Removed*). **The package file is not part of this commit.** It is exported from the content folder of the maintainers' system once `mdb content-deploy` has brought that folder to the checkout, and the four earlier files stay in the repository until the commit that adds it. Until then seven tests of `mdb` are skipped with the reason *the package is exported from the owner's system after this round* - the six that read the shipped package (every option in the Prompt Builder report, its neutral defaults, no per-user state and no host in a report, the committed `dist/index.html` in the UI job, no rule, no share and no retired UI, and the package equal to the repository) and the one that wants the four earlier files gone -, `mdb package-check` says that the checkout holds no package and ends with exit code 1, the workflow runs it with `--allow-missing`, and `mdb builders-import` ends with exit code 2. The commands that write and read the package ran on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12, as they were then, in a test content root below the folder of a user and on packages exported from it; the entries of the commands say what was measured there, what it led to, and what has not run since. See *The content folder* in *Deploying the Builder UIs* of the Administration Guide.
- **`mdb content-deploy` brings a content folder to the state of a checkout.** From the manifest it creates the folders, uploads the files, registers the custom steps and updates a step that exists in place with `PUT /dataFlows/steps/{id}`, so that the step keeps its id and saved flows keep working, and creates the job definitions or updates them in place - the program as `code`, the page of the Prompt Builder as the property `form`, the parameters. `--content-root` names another folder than `/SAS Agentic AI Accelerator`, `--only <path>` a part of the list, `--dry-run` writes nothing. One line per entry says `equal`, `updated`, `created` or `FAILED` with the reason, and a run with a failure ends with exit code 1. A report is not deployed: it is authored in SAS Visual Analytics and reaches a site with the package. What sits in a folder of the list and is not listed is named and left alone, and nothing is deleted. A step is compared in its program, its form and its ports; a change of its description alone is not deployed. The parameters of a job definition are written as the manifest has them, except the two that are settings of a site, `_contextName` and `contentRoot` (see *Changed*). Measured on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12, with the command as it was then, in a test content root below the folder of a user. The dry run listed the 72 entries - the 61 objects and their eleven folders - as `70 created, 2 not deployed`, the two reports, and wrote nothing: the folder did not exist afterwards. The run created the 70, and read back from the folder every repository file had the bytes of the repository with LF line ends and every custom step and job definition equalled its source in each part that is compared. A second run said `70 equal, 2 not deployed`, and a listing of the folder before and after it differed in no id and no modified time. A file, a custom step and a job definition were then changed by hand: the dry run named the three as `updated` and wrote nothing, the run updated them, and the step and the job definition had the ids they had before. The file had a new id, because the command of that day deleted a file that differed and uploaded it again; it replaces the content in place since (see *Changed*). Five objects the manifest does not list - a file, a folder with a file in it, a registered step and a job definition - kept their ids and modified times through every run, and four of them were listed under `Not in the manifest and left alone`: of a folder that is not listed the folder is named, not what it holds. Against the content folder of the test system the dry run said `16 equal, 34 updated, 20 created, 2 not deployed` and wrote nothing. Not run against SAS Viya: what came after that pass - the write of a file in place, the stop at the first write that fails, the settings of a site and `--to`.
- **`release.json` and `Data/ACCELERATOR_RELEASES.csv` in the content folder.** `mdb content-deploy` writes both. `release.json` holds the release, the commit of the checkout the folder was deployed from with the date of that commit, and every entry of the manifest with its kind and - for a file, a custom step and a job definition - a SHA-256 hash of its content; the page *Start Here* reads it. The CSV is the release table byte for byte as `mdb load-releases --csv` writes it, and the job `Load-Accelerator-Tables` loads it. Both are made from the checkout, the CSV from `CHANGELOG.md`, and `mdb package-check` compares them with what the checkout gives now: after a change to the changelog the package differs until the folder is deployed and exported again - a line that starts with `drift:` between two releases, an error for a release (see *Changed*). Measured on 2026-10-12 in the test content root: the CSV in the folder was byte for byte the file `mdb load-releases --csv` wrote, 626,101 bytes and 707 rows, with the CRLF line ends that file has; `release.json` held release 2.3.0, the commit of the checkout and 72 entries, and the SHA-256 of `RAG/rag_core/steps.py` as downloaded from the folder was the hash of its entry.
- **`mdb options-save` carries the retired RAG Builder report into the Prompt Builder.** It reads a live report named `RAG Builder` when one exists and folds its options into the `Prompt Builder` section (see the upgrade steps for the rules); `mdb options-restore` and `mdb builders-import --options` fold a file saved with 2.2.x the same way and never write to the retired report. Every line the fold decides is printed: what was carried, which shared value was kept, which values of the old report were not carried because the Prompt Builder report already holds others - one line per retired report, `N value(s) differ`, with the values - and whether `enableRag` was set and why. A report that lacks an option of the 2.3.0 report is an older one: both commands write what it can hold, name the options it cannot, and end with exit code 1. A report that rejected a value - a checkbox given a word that says neither on nor off - is named with that value and ends the command with exit code 1 as well; `mdb options-restore` no longer prints `already matches` under that line. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 with the Prompt Builder report of this release, after the report had been saved once in SAS Visual Analytics with **Enable RAG setups** ticked: in a round of `mdb options-save` and `mdb options-restore`, the dry run of the restore named six options to write and none that the report lacks, and the restore wrote them. One call of `mdb` that day ended with `401 Client Error` at the sign-in and went through when it was repeated - the sign-in service of that system refused password grants that overlapped that day; repeat the command before looking for another cause.
- **`mdb builders-import` imports the one package and keeps the options of the Prompt Builder report without a file.** `SAS-Viya-Integrations/SAS-Agentic-AI-Accelerator.json` is the default; a checkout without it gets the message that it holds no package, and exit code 2. Without `--options`, and when the package carries the Prompt Builder report and that report or the retired report `RAG Builder` is on the site, the command reads the options before the import, keeps them in `builder-options-captured-<time>.json` in the current directory and writes them back afterwards - so an upgrade is one command. The file holds option values and no key. `--options` writes back a file saved with `mdb options-save`, as before, and on a site without the report the values `mdb setup` discovers are written and RAG stays off. A package without the Prompt Builder report leaves the report on the site as it is. An import removes nothing: a report `RAG Builder` that is still on the site is reported on every run, with how to remove it. A run that did not do everything ends with exit code 1 and the exact command to repeat, the kept file and the flags of the run included, on a line of its own, so that it can be selected and pasted whatever the width of the terminal: paths are spelled with forward slashes, and an argument a shell would read something into is quoted for PowerShell and bash. `--dry-run` lists the objects and what a run would do and imports nothing; it sums up the options that arrive with the new report in one line instead of reporting each as missing, and without `--options` it discovers nothing, because discovery creates a missing repository or project. Measured on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12 with the command as it was then, which handed the whole package to the SAS Viya transfer service, and with a package without reports that `mdb package-export` had written from a test content root below the folder of a user, imported on the system it was exported from. The dry run listed the objects and imported nothing. The import ended with `imported 71 object(s).`, `Done.` and exit code 0, and two things had gone wrong. First, the content was no longer in the folder it was exported from: the transfer service had created a new folder with the name of that content root at the root of SAS Content, and the five sub-folders of the test root, with everything in them, now had it as their parent. The package holds no folder above its root, and the service takes no target folder: a copy of the package that was rewritten for a second test root - the mapping the service returned for it held a version and nothing to name a folder with - arrived in a second new folder at the root of SAS Content, and the folder it was meant for stayed empty. Whether an export that keeps the folders above its root lands elsewhere was not tried. Second, a file that was in its folder under the same name with another id - one file, in a folder that itself had the id the package names - was not replaced: it kept its 67 bytes and its id, the file of the package existed under the id of the package in no folder, the task said `was updated but cannot be added to the intended folder` and `An item named "tokens.py" of type "File" already exists in the folder "rag_core"`, three of the 71 tasks ended `completedWithWarnings`, and the job said `completed` with `errorCount` 0. What was on the system under the ids of the package was written in place: 18 custom steps, 35 files and three job definitions, each with the id it had, a step and a job definition that had been changed by hand among them. These two facts are why the command no longer leaves the content to the transfer service and takes a content root for everything but the reports (see *Changed*). Not measured: a custom step or a job definition of the same name under another id; an import of a package whose root folder is at the root of SAS Content, which is how every release so far was installed; and the capture and the write-back of the options, the import mapping of a report and the commands file, which need a system whose Prompt Builder report may be written.
- **`mdb package-check` compares the package with the checkout.** Every entry of the manifest has to be in the package and equal its source - a file its repository file, line ends aside; a generated file what `mdb` generates now, `release.json` by its entries; a custom step the `.step` file in `templates`, `ui` and `flowMetadata`; a job definition its program, its form and its parameters -, nothing else may be in it, there may be no authorization rule and no share, and a report must name no host, carry no per-user state (each is a full copy of the report with a user id) and pass the checks of its entry: for the Prompt Builder report every option the UI reads, with its neutral default in every copy, and for both reports the CAS tables they bind. One line per difference; `--fix` rewrites hosts, states and option defaults in the file. For a release every difference is an error, and between two releases a difference in content is a line that starts with `drift:` and does not fail (see *Changed*). The test suite of `mdb` asks the same of the shipped package, and the workflow that runs both starts on a change to every file the manifest names as a source - a test fails for a source no path of the workflow covers -, so a rebuilt `dist/index.html` fails until the folder is deployed and the package exported again. Run over the four packages of earlier releases, the comparison found what a reading of them had found: nine stale steps and nine stale files in the RAG package, the stale UI form and optimization program, 16 missing option labels and three per-user states in the Prompt Builder package, and the rule and the share in the package of the usage report. Measured on 2026-10-12 on packages `mdb package-export` wrote from the test content root: for an export without the two reports the command printed the two lines that say so and ended with exit code 1; for an export of the folder with copies of the two reports of the test system in it, `every entry of the manifest is in the package and equals the checkout`, exit code 0; and for an export after a file, a custom step and a job definition had been changed by hand it named six differences - the `ui` of the step, the file with its two sizes, `code` and `parameters` of the job definition and the two reports. The package that passed held the four pictures of the usage report, which the command of that day did not look at; they are compared since (see *Fixed*).
- **`mdb content-deploy --only <manifest path> --to <path, id or URI>` updates an object a site has somewhere else.** A site whose flows use a custom step it registered in a folder of its own - the starter package of earlier releases put *LLM - Log Parser* into the folder `Custom Steps`, and a step may live in the folder of a user - has that step under an id its flows reference, and neither the import nor a deploy of the content folder touches it. `mdb content-deploy --only "Logging and Monitoring/LLM - Log Parser.step" --to "<SAS Content path, id or URI of the step>"` updates that one object in place from the checkout: it keeps its id and its name, it is never created, and no folder is made; where the target is not there, the command says `Nothing was updated.` and ends with exit code 1. It works for a custom step, a file and a job definition, and `--dry-run` says what a run would do. With a folder of the manifest, `--to` names the folder its entries go into: `mdb content-deploy --only RAG --to "/Team/Retrieval"` deploys the RAG runtime into a RAG content root of any name. Not run against SAS Viya.
- **`mdb builders-import --report-caslib <library>` binds the other tables of the reports to a CAS library of a site.** The tables the reports read beside the release table - `LLM_LOGS`, the two fact sheets, `PROMPT_EXPERIMENTS` - are pointed at the tables of the same name in that library through the import mapping; without the option they stay as exported, in `Public`. The release table follows `--caslib` / `SAS_CAS_LIBRARY` as before. The option takes the place of the search and replace in the package file that the guide had for the usage report. Not run against SAS Viya: the pass of 2026-10-12 imported no report.
- **`mdb builders-import` writes the authorization commands for the content folder.** The package carries no authorization rule and no share - the package of the usage report carried one of each, naming users. The command writes `sas-viya-cli-content-commands.txt` (`--out` names the directory, `--no-files` leaves the file out): two `sas-viya authorization create-rule` commands that give the *LLM Consumers* group Read on the content folder and on what is in it, with the id of the folder filled in. It runs neither of them. The rules `mdb setup` writes cover the model repository and not this folder. Not run against SAS Viya: the imports of the pass of 2026-10-12 ran with `--no-files`.
- **Start Here: one page with the links and an installation check.** The job definition `Start Here` at the root of the content folder (program `SAS-Viya-Integrations/Setup/Start-Here.sas`) writes one HTML page: what the accelerator is, links to the Prompt Builder report and the LLM Usage Report by the uri they have on the system, links to the guides, and an installation check. Every line is `ok`, `missing` or `not checked`, with what was found and one sentence what to do: `release.json` of the content root, every entry it lists, the five CAS tables the two reports bind, the SAS Model Manager repository and the three projects of the definitions with their models, the options of the Prompt Builder report that name them, the credential domain by its name, the Python packages of each part, the publishing destinations and the compute contexts. The page changes nothing - every request is a GET - and holds no token and no user id. A service that does not answer or a right that is lacking is a line and not a failed job, and the page shows what the session that built it may read. The job definition needs the parameter `_output_type` = `html`, and the content root it checks is its parameter `contentRoot`. Measured on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12, with the program as it was then, from a test content root. Opened the way a browser opens it, with `_contextName=SAS Studio compute context`, the job answered HTTP 200 with `text/html` and no `Content-Disposition`; the page began with `<!DOCTYPE html>`, held no script, link or image element and read `97 ok, 2 missing, 1 not checked`, with release 2.3.0, the commit and its date and `70 of 72 entries of release.json are present` - the two missing ones are the reports, which a deploy does not bring. With copies of the two reports in the folder, a run through the jobExecution API logged `the page holds 102 checks - 102 ok, 0 missing, 0 not checked`; such a run has no `_webout`, the program says so in a NOTE, and no page of that run was kept. A file that was deleted was `missing | not in its folder`, a `.step` uploaded as a file in the place of a registered step `missing | a file of that name is there, and no registered step`, a content root that does not exist one `missing` line with the other sections filled, and against the content folder of the test system the page read `29 ok, 1 missing` - `release.json`, which that folder did not hold yet. A listing of the folder before and after a page showed no change, and no page held a token, a password, a host name or an e-mail address, and the user id only inside the folder path the page was given; the publishing destinations are listed by their names. Opened without `_contextName`, as the installation text of that day had it, the page checked nothing: see *Fixed*. Not measured: the page for a user without administrator rights, and the program pasted into SAS Studio. What changed in the program after the pass is in *Changed* and *Fixed*, and is not run in SAS.
- **Load-Accelerator-Tables: the tables of the reports without the command line.** The job definition `Data/Load-Accelerator-Tables` (program `SAS-Viya-Integrations/Setup/Load-Accelerator-Tables.sas`) loads `ACCELERATOR_RELEASES` from `Data/ACCELERATOR_RELEASES.csv` and the three fact sheets from the files of the `Data` folder, promoted and saved, into the CAS library of its parameter `caslib` (default `Public`). The fact sheets are loaded by `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, which the job includes from the folder `Logging and Monitoring` of the content root. The release table gets the columns and types `mdb load-releases` leaves in CAS: the three numbers DOUBLE, the eight text columns VARCHAR. A table that exists is replaced only by an upload that holds every row of its file. The job writes an ERROR line and sets `SYSCC` to 1012 when a parameter is refused or one of the four tables is not in the CAS library at its end. Measured on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12 from a test content root, with `caslib=casuser` and a release table of another name. The job as shipped ran in a folder without `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, so that it wrote no fact sheet table of the system: `the release table ... was loaded with 707 rows, promoted and saved`, the WARNING that `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` was not found, then the ERROR line that names the three fact sheet tables as not in the CAS library, `SYSCC` 1012 and a failed job - as built. A second run replaced the table; without the CSV in the folder the table was left as it was, with the WARNING; and `releaseTable=x;y` was refused with one ERROR line. With the three fact sheet names replaced by test names - three lines of the job code and fifteen of `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, nothing else - the job completed without a WARNING and with `SYSCC` 0: the release table with 707 rows and the sheets with 30, 16 and 5 rows, promoted and saved; without `decision_fact_sheet.csv` in the folder, the WARNING for that sheet and the two others loaded. The release table of that run was compared with one `mdb load-releases` loaded: 707 rows, the same eleven columns in the same order, the three numbers DOUBLE, the eight text columns VARCHAR with equal lengths, no difference in `PROC COMPARE`, and `builder_part` empty in 341 rows of both. One thing differed: the formatted length, the width CAS keeps for display, is 16 in the table of the job for `version`, `release_date`, `change_type` and `builder_part`, and 6, 10, 8 and 8 in the table of `mdb`. It is left as it is: those are the text columns whose longest value is shorter than 16 bytes, so the width cuts no value, and the page of the Prompt Builder reads nothing from the table; what an object of SAS Visual Analytics shows with it was not measured. The table that was compared came from a copy of the CSV with LF line ends that was uploaded for that run; for the job as shipped, which read the deployed file, 707 rows and equal column lengths were read. Every job log of that pass begins with three ERROR lines from the start of the SAS session about a connection to CAS on port 5570, in front of the first line of a program. Not run: the job with its own table names and `caslib=Public`.
- **Settings of the logging programs survive a new file.** `Log-Parser-Code.sas`, `Get-All-Prompts.sas`, `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas`, `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` and `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas` take a setting only where its macro variable is not set when the program starts. A site keeps its values in a program of its own that sets them and includes the shipped file, so an import or a newer file replaces none of them. The defaults are unchanged, each program still deletes its settings at its end, and the two custom steps get every setting from a control as before. A macro variable of that name that something else left in the SAS session counts as set - a run that was cancelled leaves the settings of the program behind. See *Settings that stay yours* in *Logging & Monitoring* of the Administration Guide. Measured on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12 with the parser, the pull and the link, each from a program that set its settings in front of an `%include` of the shipped file of a test content root: the parser wrote its table under the name and into the CAS library that were set - 1040 rows and 36 columns, promoted and saved, the settings that were not set at their defaults -, the pull its five tables (318 rows and 61 columns, 27 and 32, 17 and 26, 107 and 40, 421 and 21), and the link wrote `1040 of 1040 rows ... linked against 421 catalog rows`. Behind each include the settings of the program were gone - seven, eleven and twelve macro variables -, and `Public.LLM_LOGS` and the tables of the pull in `Public` had the rows and the modified times they had before. With `%let _gap_decision_project = ;` the catalog had 416 rows instead of 421; that the five are the decision models was not read. `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas` ran the same way with one run table (see *Fixed* for two), and `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` got its two settings from the job `Load-Accelerator-Tables`. The 37 defaults were compared in the code with the `%let` lines they replace, not in a run: the programs with their defaults write `Public`.
- **The verification of the Prompt Builder covers all three kinds and the options.** Beside the two existing browser suites there is one for the RAG kind in the merged page (the four steps, save, manifest, launch, ledger, retrieval test, a withheld vector database) and one that plays Visual Analytics around the page for every combination of the three switches, asserting that a switched-off feature shows nothing and requests nothing. Every score code a suite manifests is compiled by a real Python interpreter, and the prompt and decision manifests are called to check that the model sends the text the experiment sent.
- **The log parser is tested in the repository.** `SAS-Viya-Integrations/Logging-Monitoring/tests` runs the Python of the log parser outside of SAS, with stand-ins for the SAS object and for `swat`, on made-up logs - an empty file, an LLM, a decision and an embedding call, a redacted log, blocks of several containers in one file - and fails when `Log-Parser-Code.sas` and the *LLM - Log Parser* custom step no longer hold the same program or when the redaction of the guide's collection script leaves a secret behind. The workflow that verifies the model definitions runs it, and starts on a change to the log parser, the guide or the deployment templates. The tests also hold the text of the step's *About* tab and of the guide where those describe the program - the CAS session the step starts, the libref lookup, the messages it writes, the copy from SAS Content. They take the condition of the step's session check from the program and decide it for a SAS session without a CAS session, for one whose own session was ended and for one with a session of the user (see *Fixed*). What the tests do not run: the SAS part of the log parser - the CAS actions that replace, append, load, promote and save the table - of which they hold the text and the order of the actions, and the `kubectl` loop of the collection script, of which they read the text and run the `sed` expressions. Both were run on the test system on 2026-10-11, see *The log collection and the log parser were measured on SAS Viya*.
- **The log collection and the log parser were measured on SAS Viya.** On the test system (SAS Viya 2026.09, 2026-10-11) the collection script of the guide, `Log-Parser-Code.sas` and the *LLM - Log Parser* step were run, the parser as SAS Job Execution jobs that wrote to a table in `CASUSER`. The script, with only its log path and interval changed, wrote 5,773 lines with 174 requests; an unredacted copy of the same logs held one provider key and seven `Cookie` headers, and the written file held `API_KEY:REDACTED` once and `Cookie: REDACTED` seven times, neither the key nor one of the cookie values, and the same number of lines. The 174 requests gave 174 rows: 57 of three LLMs, 50 of a decision model with its questions and answers as JSON, 67 of an embedding model with its tokens in `prompt_length`. A file of 0 bytes and a file with start-up lines only ended with the note and left the table and its saved file as they were, in replace and in append mode. Two newer collections took the table from 174 to 195 to 202 rows; before the second the table was dropped from memory while its `.sashdat` stayed, and the parser loaded the saved table first. A log collected with `kubectl logs --timestamps` gave the warning for unread requests with the count of its 26 `Request: POST` lines. A request that was still running where its container's part of the file ended, and two requests that failed in the container, each gave a row without token counts, run time and response. The step, run as the code SAS Studio generated for a flow, wrote the same rows as the program (174 replacing, 195 after an append). Measured later the same day, in eleven more SAS Job Execution jobs of the *SAS Studio compute context*, with the step as it was before its session check was changed (see *Fixed*) and the same 174 requests. In jobs whose session had no CAS session the step started `LLMLOGMYSESS`, wrote the table and ended the session: promoted and saved, the table was read from a new CAS session with its 174 rows; saved and not promoted, no table was in memory and the `.sashdat` held the 174 rows; neither, nothing was left. In a job whose session had a CAS session and no libref of the output table the step wrote the same promoted and saved table. The copy block of the guide ran as it is printed there, with only the folder filled in - `Copy of llms.log: rc=0 msg=`, 558,176 bytes with the MD5 digest of the upload -, and `Log-Parser-Code.sas` behind it, with its own `_log_path` line set to the copy, wrote the 174 rows. A log file that is missing, in replace mode: `ERROR: Unhandled Python exception.` with a `FileNotFoundError`, then `ERROR: The log could not be parsed, the table … was left as it is.`, `SYSCC` 1012 and a job that ended as failed; no table of that name existed before the run, so what was seen is that none was created. Not measured: the redaction of the `Authorization` and `X-CSRF-TOKEN` headers (none occurred), the shared mount of the guide's example paths and the hourly crontab entry, the other error branches (a saved file that can not be loaded, a `<table>_NEW` that can not be built), append without promoting, a target caslib other than `CASUSER`, the step clicked in the SAS Studio UI, a log with bytes that are not valid UTF-8 and two overlapping requests in one container. The session check of this release was measured after these runs, see *Fixed*. See *What Was Measured* in *Logging & Monitoring* of the Administration Guide.
- **The Logging & Monitoring guide says how the collected file reaches the SAS compute server.** The path the collection script writes to (`/viya-share/pvs/sasdata/data/llm/llms.log`) and the default `_log_path` of the log parser (`sasserver:/export/pvs/sasdata/data/llm/llms.log`) are examples of one site: they name the same file only where the collecting host and the SAS compute server mount the same storage under these two paths. The guide now says so and adds the way that was measured on the test system, where a compute session has neither `/export` nor `/viya-share`: the redacted file is uploaded to a folder in SAS Content with the Files service, and the job that runs the parser first copies it into the compute session with a `FILESRVC` file reference and `fcopy`. `fcopy` returned 0, the copy of the first collection had the size and the MD5 digest of the collected file, and a file of 0 bytes arrived as one. The three steps were started one after the other, not from a crontab entry. `Log-Parser-Code.sas` is not changed by this. The last line of the copy block, `%let _log_path = sasserver:/tmp/llms.log;`, is the `_log_path` line of the program, changed - not one more line in front of it. The guide said only that `Log-Parser-Code.sas` *follows*; measured on 2026-10-11, the block in front of the unchanged program copied the file and the program then read its own default path: `FileNotFoundError`, `ERROR: The log could not be parsed, …` and a failed job. With the program's own line set to the copy, the same block gave the 174 rows. The guide now says which line is meant.
- **The *LLM - Log Parser* step says what it takes from the SAS session.** The step is `Log-Parser-Code.sas` without the first lines that start a CAS session and assign the librefs. Its *About* tab, the README of `Logging-Monitoring` and the guide now say what it does instead: it asks the `SESSFOUND` function whether the session that the option `SESSREF` names is connected, runs in that session when it is and otherwise starts a session of its own (`llmLogMySess`) that it ends as its last action, so that of such a run only a promoted or saved table is left; it reads the caslib of the output table from its libref in `dictionary.libnames`, and the name of a libref that is not assigned is handed to CAS as the name of the caslib; it stages the rows as `_TEMP_LLM_LOGS` in the `CASUSER` of the user; its Python connects to CAS with the options `CASHOST` and `CASPORT` and the environment variables `SSLCALISTLOC` and `SAS_SERVICES_TOKEN`. The code SAS Studio generated for a flow of the step and its output table held no `cas` and no `libname` statement. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, in SAS Job Execution jobs of the *SAS Studio compute context*, with the step as it was that day - its session check still went by the macro variable `_CASNAME_` (see *Fixed*): the step was put on the server with `PUT /dataFlows/steps/{id}` and kept its id, and its stored program and the texts of its *About* tab equalled the file. With a `cas` and a `libname` statement in front of the generated code, the step ran in that session. With no `cas` and no `libname` statement in front, `_CASNAME_` did not exist: the step ran `cas llmLogMySess;`, wrote, promoted and saved the table, and ended the session - `NOTE: Deletion of the session LLMLOGMYSESS was successful.`, `NOTE: The default CAS session LLMLOGMYSESS identified by SAS option SESSREF= was terminated. Use the OPTIONS statement to set the SESSREF= option to an active session.`, `NOTE: Request to TERMINATE completed for session LLMLOGMYSESS.` Afterwards `_CASNAME_`, `_SESSREF_` and the option `SESSREF` still named `LLMLOGMYSESS`, while `cas _all_ list;` listed no session. With a `cas` statement and no libref in front, the step ran in that session and wrote the table to `CASUSER`, the caslib of the libref's name. In that job the lines SAS Studio adds behind the step reported the output table as not existing - `_FLW_ACTION_TABLE_|CASUSER|<table>|0`, where the job with the libref had `|1` - although the table was there, promoted and saved with its 174 rows; the guide names this beside the libref. The guide also says that lines before the first source line of a job log are not the parser's: on the test system every job log of the *SAS Studio compute context* began with two CAS connect `ERROR` messages there - on three log lines, all typed as errors, because the second message is longer than the line -, also in a job without parser code. The session check that asks `SESSFOUND` was measured in further jobs later that day (see *Fixed*). The step was not opened or clicked in SAS Studio, so how the *About* tab shows there was not looked at.
- **`LLM_LOGS` holds every kind of request with what tells them apart: 24 columns behind the twelve it had.** `Log-Parser-Code.sas` and the *LLM - Log Parser* step (version 2.4) keep the twelve columns with their names, order, types and meanings - the *LLM Usage Report* reads them - and add: `kind` (`llm`, `embedding`, `decision`, by the names of the inputs), `status_code`, `failed`, `error_text` (the first ERROR line, at most 500 characters, keys replaced), `transaction_id`, `request_id` (the `X-Request-Id` header), `user_agent` (`python-requests`, `browser`, `SAS` or `other` - no other header, no address and no Referer is read), `embedding_mode`, `project`, `input_hash` (SHA-256 of the embedded text; the text itself stays out of the table as before), `answer` and `answer_confidence` (the headline of a decision), `options` (as sent, with the key of a provider and every other value that is a key by its name or its shape replaced by `REDACTED` by the parser itself, whether or not the log was redacted - also inside a value: the user and the password of an address, a header that carries a token, what follows `bearer` or `basic`), `rag_collection` and `rag_query_hash` (for an LLM whose user prompt holds what a search of a document collection returned: the collection and the SHA-256 of the question, decoded from the JSON object in the prompt that has all six keys of such a result, the last one where a prompt holds several), and nine columns the parser writes empty for the program that links the logs to SAS Model Manager (`model_id`, `caller_model_id`, `caller_project_id`, `caller_kind`, `caller_name`, `experiment_run_id`, `rag_model_id`, `match_method`, `match_candidates`). A hash is taken of the text with every run of white space made one blank, so the question that was asked of a collection has the hash of the embedding call that embedded it. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11, with the program as it was that day (version 2.2 of the step), on a redacted log with 1040 requests: 1040 rows and 36 columns, 24 of them VARCHAR, promoted and saved, without an ERROR, a WARNING or a NOTE; 437 embedding, 355 decision and 248 LLM rows; 787 with status 200 and 253 failed; 238 of the 248 LLM rows with a collection, and each of the 238 question hashes equal to the `input_hash` of an embedding row in query mode; the first twelve columns equal to those the parser wrote for the same file before it had the further columns (version 2.1 of the step), except `max_tokens` in 5 rows; and the step, run as the code SAS Studio generated for a flow, wrote the same table. On 2026-10-12 the parser of that day (version 2.3 of the step) ran on the same log on the same system: 1040 rows and 36 columns again, without a message, with the same counts per kind, status and failed rows; against a parse of the same file with the program of 2026-10-11, 25 of the 26 columns that were compared were equal in every row, and `rag_query_hash` differed in 41 rows, whose prompts hold more than one such result and get the question hash of the last - all 238 hashes were still the `input_hash` of an embedding row in query mode; the step in a flow wrote the same table, and one run wrote it to `Public`. What the parser does since that run - version 2.4 of the step, see *Fixed* - ran on the test system later that day and gave the same 1040 rows in all 36 columns for that log (see *The three programs and the ingestion job ran once more on SAS Viya* below). Version 2.5 of the step is not run in SAS. *Logging & Monitoring* lists the columns under *The Columns of LLM_LOGS*.
- **`Get-All-Prompts.sas` pulls the experiments of every kind, and every row carries the SAS Model Manager id of its model and of its project.** The program read the projects tagged `Prompt-Engineering` and of each model the file `Prompt-Experiment-Tracker.json`; it now also reads `Prompt-Optimization-Tracker.json`, the manifested score code, the projects tagged `RAG-Engineering` with their RAG setups, and the projects that hold the definitions of the LLMs, the embedding models and the decision models. It writes five CAS tables, each dropped, loaded, promoted and saved, and each with `modelID` and `projectID`: `PROMPT_EXPERIMENTS` (one row per run and model, and one row for a model without a run; key `modelID`, `runID`, `model`), `PROMPT_OPTIMIZATIONS` (one row per optimization or comparison; key `modelID`, `optimizationId`), `RAG_SETUPS` (one row per setup, saved by the Prompt Builder or registered by the step *RAG - Register Setup*; key `modelID`), `RAG_RUNS` (the rows of every setup's `<tables_prefix>_RUNS` table that is in memory, and for a setup without one the runs its `<tables_prefix>_LEDGER` table still names, with `run_source` saying which; key `modelID`, `run_id`) and `MODEL_CATALOG` (one row per model and pattern; key `modelID`, `role`, `runID`, `optimization_id`, `target_model`). The output CAS library and every table name are `%let` lines at the top, with `Public` and these names as defaults. A table name is a SAS name of 32 characters at most: any other ends the program with one ERROR line before anything is read. An output without rows is written as well, with all its columns: the DATA step that builds it declares the columns and reads nothing. The step *RAG - Load Vector Store* publishes a run table, and from this release on the ingestion job of a setup of the Prompt Builder does as well (see *Changed*). A setup whose job has not published one - a job definition from before, or a run history that is switched off - has its ledger only, and a ledger names the newest run only: for such a setup `RAG_RUNS` holds that run, from the second its id holds to the latest of its rows, without counts. The ledger is read only where the run table is not in memory, so a run that both name is one row. A setup with neither table in memory is left out with a NOTE; no table of a setup is loaded or changed. Every listing of SAS Model Manager is read to its last page. A model whose file can not be read, and a project or model that can not be listed, is named in a WARNING and the pull goes on; when the projects themselves can not be listed the program ends with an ERROR and leaves the output tables as they are. The run ends with one NOTE per output table, with its rows and columns. Nothing is written to SAS Model Manager. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11 with the program as it was that day: 41 projects with 92 models and 10 projects with 17 RAG setups were read in 17 to 20 seconds, and the five tables were written, promoted and saved - 318 rows and 61 columns, 27 and 32, 17 and 26, 105 and 39, 365 and 15 -, the keys of the experiments and of the catalog unique. The table names it refuses, the runs it takes from a ledger, the further columns and roles of the catalog and the session check of the code file were added after that run and ran on 2026-10-12: the five tables with 318 rows and 61 columns, 27 and 32, 17 and 26, 107 and 40, 421 and 21, in 18.6 seconds, all four keys unique; a table name of 34 characters refused with its ERROR line before anything was read or written; 105 rows of `RAG_RUNS` from four run tables and 2 from ledgers - for the setup the Prompt Builder had saved one run of six seconds, with its ledger unchanged; three WARNINGs for setups without a collection; and no second start of `mysess` behind the parser. That refusal ended as a completed job; since then a run that wrote an ERROR line and left an output table unwritten sets `SYSCC` to 1012 (see *Fixed*), and later that day the same refusal ended as a failed job. The Python of the program runs in the test suite against a made-up SAS Model Manager.
- **`MODEL_CATALOG` says what each prompt template, decision template and RAG setup sends, and which models answer.** For a prompt template and a decision template it holds one row with the templates (`runID` empty): what the manifested score code sends - read from the code, which is parsed and never run - or, for a model without such score code, the templates of its newest run that is marked as the best prompt, or of its newest run; `template_source` says which. For a decision template the system template is the questions and the user template the state. It holds one row per experiment run and model with the templates and the prompts as they were sent - the `{{variables}}` filled in the way the Prompt Builder does it, a decision run with its questions as stored - and the hash of each, taken of the whole text before it is cut to the length of its column, and with what the tracker holds of that call: its run time and its token counts (`run_time`, `prompt_length`, `output_length`) - the run time tells the request of a run from another request with the same prompts; one row per RAG setup that names a collection, with its embedding model and in `template_source` who wrote the model, `builder` or `step` (`role` `rag`); one row per run that was judged and model that judged it, with the prompts of the run, which the Prompt Builder shows a judge in the user prompt of its call (`role` `judge`); one row per optimization and model it called, with the start and the end of the job in UTC (`window_start`, `window_end`), its id (`optimization_id`) and the system prompt it started from (`role` `optimization`); and one row per LLM, embedding model and decision model (`role` `llm`, `embedding`, `decision_model`), whose `name` is the name of its container. The definition projects are found by name (`LLM Model Project`, `Embedding Model Project`, `Decision Model Project`), and each setting also takes a project id or `tag:<tag>`, several separated by `|`; a project that is not found is a WARNING, an empty setting leaves the kind out. Measured outside of SAS on files saved from the test system: the templates were read from nine manifested score codes, one of them a decision template manifested with 2.2.0 whose score code does not compile and is read statement by statement; the questions and the state template read from the score code of one decision template fit 41 of the 105 requests to its decision model in a collected log of 1,040 requests, and the questions of its tracker equalled the questions of those requests character by character. On the test system on 2026-10-11 the catalog of that day - fifteen columns, no rows for judges and optimizations - was written with 365 rows: the prompts as sent of 70 runs and models of two templates were found in the collected log by their hashes, and a setup whose properties SAS Model Manager did not return (HTTP 500) had a row without a collection. Such a setup is left out of the catalog since, with a WARNING. The six further columns, the two further roles and that rule ran on the test system on 2026-10-12: the catalog had 21 columns and 421 rows - 285 `prompt`, 47 `decision`, 24 `judge`, 35 `optimization`, 14 `rag`, 8 `llm`, 3 `embedding` and 5 `decision_model` -, its key was unique, `run_time`, `prompt_length` and `output_length` were numbers in CAS, and three setups without a collection were left out with a WARNING each. An `optimizationId` that a tracker holds on two entries gets a counter in the catalog since (see *Fixed*, with what was measured of it later that day).
- **`Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` links every log row to the model that answered and to the prompt template, decision template or RAG setup that sent the request.** A new program in `SAS-Viya-Integrations/Logging-Monitoring` fills the link columns of the log table (`model_id`, `caller_model_id`, `caller_project_id`, `caller_kind`, `caller_name`, `experiment_run_id`, `rag_model_id`, `match_method`, `match_candidates`) with SAS Model Manager ids. It reads the log table, the table `MODEL_CATALOG` that `Get-All-Prompts.sas` writes and, when it is there, `RAG_RUNS`; all three are macro variables at the top of the program (defaults `Public.LLM_LOGS`, `Public.MODEL_CATALOG`, `Public.RAG_RUNS`). The model that answered is the catalog row whose name is the name of the container. No request and no log line names its caller, so every other link is made by content, in this order: the prompts of an experiment run of the Builder, compared by their hashes, for a request a browser can have sent and whose run time is the run time the tracker holds for the run - a run is given to one request (`experiment run`); the questions of a decision template with or without a state that fits its state template (`questions and state`, `questions`); the templates of a prompt with every `{{variable}}` standing for any text (`template`); a call the Prompt Builder made for a prompt - a request that holds the text of a catalog row of an optimization or of a judge, went to the model of that row and, for an optimization, was sent inside its window (`optimization`, `judge`; a judge call also gets the run it judged); for embedding rows the query of a retrieval that an LLM row carries within `_lnk_query_window` seconds behind it (`retrieval query`, 300 seconds by default) and the window of an ingestion run (`ingestion run`). An LLM row whose prompt carries a retrieval also gets the RAG setup of its collection in `rag_model_id`, beside its prompt. A row that fits two callers equally well is `ambiguous`, a row that fits nothing is `none`; a template takes part with at least 20 characters of fixed text (`_lnk_min_literal`); where a setup saved in the Builder and the model the step *RAG - Register Setup* registered for it hold one collection, the setup of the Builder is taken. The program links the rows without a `match_method` and leaves every other row as it is; `_lnk_redo_all = 1` links all rows again, and a run that links no row leaves the table, its promotion and its saved file as they are. The twelve columns of every release and every column the program does not know stay in CAS and are not rewritten: the rows are numbered in a copy in `CASUSER`, only the link columns are uploaded and merged back by that number, and the new table replaces the old one the way the parser replaces a table - built as `<table>_NEW`, given the label of the table, promoted and saved; a table that is promoted or has a saved file stays so whatever `_lnk_promote_table` and `_lnk_save_table` say. A log table without the link columns is refused with one ERROR line that names the parser, a missing catalog with one ERROR line that names `Get-All-Prompts.sas`. A row or a catalog row whose content can not be read gets no link and is named in a WARNING, and so are the rows that no longer find the experiment run they were linked to: the id of a run is its place in the tracker, and the Prompt Builder numbers the runs again when one is deleted. The program ran on the test system on 2026-10-11 and on 2026-10-12, each time as it was that day, and was changed by what each run showed - see the next entry. The guide names what cannot be linked and why, how the tables join, and the way to exact links that is not built: a caller id that the manifested models send themselves.
- **The chain of the three programs was measured on SAS Viya.** On the test system on 2026-10-11 the parser, the pull and the link program as they were that day ran one after the other in 23 SAS Job Execution jobs of the *SAS Studio compute context*, on tables of their own in `CASUSER` and a redacted log of 1040 requests; the CAS server of that system runs without workers. For the parse see the entry of `LLM_LOGS` above and the append under *Changed*, for the pull its entry above. The link: `1040 of 1040 rows ... were linked against 365 catalog rows in 0.09 seconds`, and every row got a `model_id`. Counted in the table, the LLM rows were `experiment run` 32, `template` 144 and `none` 72, the decision rows `experiment run` 50, `questions and state` 301 and `none` 4, the embedding rows `retrieval query` 239, `ingestion run` 3, `ambiguous` 72 and `none` 123. The table kept its 36 columns with their order, types and labels, the order of its rows and every value of its other 27 columns - also a table of 3120 rows, which takes two reads. A second run linked 0 rows and a run with `_lnk_redo_all = 1` wrote the same links; the two refusals left their table untouched; tables that were saved and not in memory were loaded. For all 769 linked rows the caller was compared with the content of the row afterwards, outside of SAS, and was right. The three statements of the guide's example ran, and the joins counted what the link columns say: 527 rows to `PROMPT_EXPERIMENTS`, 242 and 238 to `RAG_SETUPS`, 1040 to the answering rows of the catalog. What did not hold on 2026-10-11, each with the change that was made and with what the changed programs did on the same system on 2026-10-12, in 35 jobs: (1) the three summary lines of the link held semicolons, which ended the statement a line is written to the SAS log with, so the rest ran as SAS code - four `ERROR 180-322` in each of the seven runs that reached the summary, and a job that ended as failed although the table was written correctly; every message is now made one text on its way, and the summary is worded without a semicolon - on 2026-10-12 no log held an `ERROR 180-322`, the job of the link ended as completed, and the summary lines arrived in full: 32, 144, 49, 13 and 10 LLM rows by `experiment run`, `template`, `optimization`, `judge` and `none`, 41, 310 and 4 decision rows by `experiment run`, `questions and state` and `none`, and 138, 3, 72 and 224 embedding rows by `retrieval query`, `ingestion run`, `ambiguous` and `none`. (2) Every request with the prompts of a run got the newest run with these prompts: 82 rows for 70 runs and models, three of them with the id of another run; a row now gets the run whose run time is its own, and a run is given to one row - on 2026-10-12 73 rows for 73 runs and models, each with the run time the tracker holds for it. (3) A query embedding was linked through any prompt with its question, 18 of them through prompts that had been sent 20 minutes to two hours before; the prompt now has to follow within `_lnk_query_window` seconds - on 2026-10-12 138 of the 290 query embeddings were linked, where 239 had been; of 84 that were sent before the first prompt of their collection 83 were `none`, and one, sent 178 seconds before a run of the Prompt Builder with the same text, is still linked. (4) The 49 calls of a prompt optimization and the 13 calls of a judge were `none`; they are now linked to their prompt - on 2026-10-12 49 and 13, and 10 LLM rows stayed `none`.
