(5) A second run with 0 rows linked built, promoted and saved the table again; it now leaves the table as it is - on 2026-10-12 with one NOTE, the same modified time and the same saved file. (6) The label of the table was lost by the link and by an append; both now give the new table the label - on 2026-10-12 the table had its label after the append and after the link, and a label of a site's own stayed after the append; the link was not run on such a table. (7) A table name of 34 characters for an output of the pull ended in `ERROR: The table ... was not written.` for a table that had been written, promoted and saved; such a name is now refused before anything is read - on 2026-10-12 with its ERROR line, and no table was written. (8) In one SAS session `Get-All-Prompts.sas` and the second run of the link wrote `WARNING: A session with the name MYSESS already exists.`, and `Get-All-Prompts.sas` ended the session of the parser; the three programs now ask `SESSFOUND` (see *Fixed*) - on 2026-10-12 parser, pull, parser, pull, link and link ran in one SAS session without that warning, also behind a CAS session of the user, which was still connected at the end. (9) The run table of the setup the Prompt Builder had saved did not exist - its ingestion job published a ledger only -, so none of its document embeddings was linked to an ingestion run: of the 147 document embeddings 3 were, 72 were `ambiguous` inside runs of run tables that several models registered by the step share, and 72 were `none`; the pull now takes the runs a ledger still names, which is the newest run only - on 2026-10-12 one run of six seconds for that setup, in which the log holds no document embedding, so no link changed; from this release on the ingestion job publishes its run table itself (see *Changed*), which was measured later that day with a setup of its own (see the entry below). Checked outside of SAS after the second run: all 730 linked rows against the texts, run times, windows and runs of the catalog, none of them wrong; one link is doubtful - the query embedding of 178 seconds named above, which is linked through a prompt that is not its own. What did not hold in the second run, each with what the programs do since - which ran later that day, see the entry below: (a) the pull's refusal of a table name and the link's refusal of a table of twelve columns ended with `SYSCC` 0 and a completed job; a run that wrote an ERROR line and no table now sets `SYSCC` to 1012. (b) With an engine other than CAS the parser wrote four ERROR lines, the last about a log it had not read; it now ends with one line. (c) A response that held a request line with a line of text behind it gave a second row, a case that was not named before; it is named now, and the row is still written. (d) Files made by hand whose times were not in file order gave `failed` = 1 for answered requests without a NOTE, and one request the numbers of another; the next part of a file now begins where the time goes back by more than a second, and a request the lines of its container end on has `failed` missing. See *Fixed* for each. Not measured: the default table names - one chain of 2026-10-12 wrote to `Public` under names of its own -, a CAS server with workers, the *LLM Usage Report* opened on the tables, and what the programs do since the pass of the entry below. See *The Chain on SAS Viya* in *Logging & Monitoring* of the Administration Guide.
- **The three programs and the ingestion job ran once more on SAS Viya, as they were after the second run.** On the test system on 2026-10-12 - SAS Viya 2026.09, with version 2.4 of the *LLM - Log Parser* step, version 2.2 of the *LLM - Get All Prompts* step, `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` and `Ingest-Documents.sas` as they were then - 46 SAS Job Execution jobs ran, six ingestions and 40 jobs of the chain, on tables of their own in `CASUSER`. (1) The ingestion job published its run history in three runs, left it alone with the history switched off and with a `rag_core` of before the change: see *The RAG ingestion job publishes the run history to CAS* under *Changed*. (2) The link found those runs. A log of the model containers of 24 hours was collected with the script of the guide - 1027 requests, every `API_KEY` value and every header value redacted - and parsed into 1027 rows; the pull wrote four rows of `RAG_RUNS` for the setup, all from its run table, with a key that was unique; and the link gave `ingestion run` to exactly four embedding rows: the three document embeddings of the first run and the one of the run with a changed document - the `chunks_written` of the two runs -, each with one candidate and the setup as its caller. The six embeddings in query mode of those minutes, one per run of the job, stayed `none`. With the run table dropped from memory the pull took one run from the ledger, the newest, and a link with `_lnk_redo_all = 1` left the four rows `none`. (3) A run that is refused ends its job as failed, and the first DATA step behind it does not run: see *A run of the log parser, of `Get-All-Prompts.sas` or of `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` that is refused no longer leaves its job completed* under *Fixed*. (4) The numbers of the second run held: the collected log of 1040 requests gave 1040 rows and 36 columns, with no row that differed in any of the 26 compared columns from a parse with the parser of the second run; the pull wrote its five tables with 318, 27, 17, 107 and 421 rows; the link gave the same counts for every method; parser, pull, parser, pull, link and link in one job ran in one CAS session; and both steps were registered, updated with `PUT` under the id they had, and ran as the code SAS Studio generates for a flow. Not run in that pass: `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, which had no setting for the names of its tables. A check of the saved logs of the pass against its report carried every measurement and corrected four statements of the report: the number of pods the log was collected from is in no saved output; the log holds ten requests to the embedding model in the minutes of the runs, not nine; the compute context is reported by the service for the six ingestion jobs and was the argument of the request for the 40 others, and the tables of the ingestion runs were read back in a compute session and not in jobs; and the stored program of the job was compared with the file for the first ingestion run - for the five others the source lines of the job log are the program. What was changed after this pass is not run in SAS: the settings that are taken only where they are not set, versions 2.5 and 2.3 of the two steps, the counter of an `optimizationId` that passes over the id of another entry, `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, the closing NOTE of `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas` and the reason of a run history that could not be read. See *The Third Run* under *The Chain on SAS Viya* in *Logging & Monitoring* of the Administration Guide.
- **The pull, the link and the three programs in a row are tested in the repository.** Beside the tests of the log parser, `SAS-Viya-Integrations/Logging-Monitoring/tests` now runs the Python of `Get-All-Prompts.sas` against a made-up SAS Model Manager that pages its listings and can fail a request - trackers with header and model rows, a decision template with its expected answers, an optimization tracker, a `rag-setup.json`, a model registered by the step, manifested score code of both kinds, an empty system -, the Python of `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` on made-up log rows and catalog rows with a stand-in for CAS - every rule and their order, ambiguity, the minimum of fixed text, a second run and a redo, the columns the program does not know -, and the three one after the other: log lines of all three kinds become rows, tracker, setup and score code files become the catalog, and the link program is given both. The tests fail when a code file and its step no longer hold the same program, when a column the *LLM Usage Report* reads changes its name or type, when the columns of a run table differ from the ones `rag_core` writes, and when the three programs no longer hand each other the same columns, kinds, roles, default table names or the same hash. 885 tests where there were 59: 394 of the parser, 12 of the collection script, 106 of the pull, 212 of the link, 11 of the chain, and 150 in three files that read all the programs at once - every text the Python hands to SAS goes through one function (119), every statement that writes an ERROR line leads to the statement that sets `SYSCC` (20), and every statement that starts or ends a CAS session stands under the check of a connected session (11). Among them: requests whose lines interleave at every position, a line that is one to thirty seconds older than the line in front of it, two experiment runs with the same prompts, the calls of a judge and of an optimization, a query embedding with and without a prompt behind it, a table name of 33 characters, the run table of a setup as its job writes it - read like the one the step writes, and with the document that was embedded inside one of its runs -, and a stand-in for the SAS object that fails a test of the link when the program hands SAS a message that would not arrive as one text. They run the Python; none of them runs SAS.
- **The release table says which part of the Prompt Builder a change concerns.** `ACCELERATOR_RELEASES` has a new last column, `builder_part`, with the values `Prompt`, `Decision` and `RAG`. It is made for a button bar on the Changelog page of the report, with which a reader picks prompt templates, decision templates or RAG setups. A button bar shows the rows of one value, so an entry of the component `Prompt Builder` is now one row for each part it concerns - equal in every other column, in the order Prompt, Decision, RAG - and an entry that names no part concerns the Builder as a whole and is written for all three. A `RAG runtime` row carries `RAG`, once; the rows of every other component and the release intros have an empty value. Every other column means what it meant, and `release_rank` with `item_rank` still counts a change once; `component` itself changes for 14 entries of earlier releases (see *Changed*). The parts are read from the wording by keyword lists, as the component is, and the first of these decides: a section heading that names a part, for every entry under it; the steps of an upgrade and an entry that says it holds for every kind, which are written for the whole Builder; the parts the headline names - read whole, also where the column `summary` holds only the beginning of a long first sentence, and with a template named without its kind (*a prompt, a template or a RAG setup*) counting as both kinds of template; the whole Builder when the headline names the Prompt Builder and no part; else what the text names. By release, a part that did not exist yet cannot be concerned: no entry before 2.2.0 is filed under `Decision`, an entry that names no part is `Prompt` alone before 2.0.0 and `Prompt` and `RAG` from 2.0.0 to 2.1.x, and up to 2.2.x the name Prompt Builder names the prompt part. `mdb load-releases` prints the rows per part beside the number of changelog entries they stand for. The page needs the filter on `component` it has and a button bar on `builder_part`; the report with that page comes with the transfer package once the report is exported with it. The table was loaded into CAS on the test system on 2026-10-12, by `mdb load-releases` and by the job `Load-Accelerator-Tables`, each under a test name: 707 rows and eleven columns, `builder_part` empty in 341 rows, `Prompt` in 134, `Decision` in 54 and `RAG` in 178. What a Changelog page shows with it was read from the shipped package, not measured in SAS Visual Analytics. See *The release table* in the Model Definition Builder guide. Upgrading from 2.2.x: nothing by hand - the import of step 2 reloads the table.
- **The Administration Guide says how to recognise an execution that SAS Micro Analytic Service ended after 30 seconds, for a decision with a retrieval model, a prompt template and a decision template.** A decision with three model nodes that were all manifested in the Prompt Builder - the retrieval model of a RAG setup, a prompt template with the included LLM call that is given the retrieved context, and a decision template - returned, in its publishing validation on a SAS Micro Analytic Service destination, rows with **HTTP status 201, an empty error column and empty outputs of all three nodes**; the log of the validation job had no ERROR or WARNING line. That is no defect of the accelerator, and nothing in the decision or in a model has to be repaired for it: the service had ended those executions because they took longer than 30 seconds. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09. With the log level debug, the scheduling thread of the service logs `0x803fc006=tkWait()` exactly 30.0 seconds after *Scheduled work on worker thread*, followed by *Failure encountered during execution in revision … of module context … Return code: 0x8b3ff145* and *Micro Analytic Service failed to execute method*; a direct REST call of the step of the module answers HTTP status 201 with `"executionState": "timedOut"` and `"outputs": []`. The publishing validation sends the rows of its input table at the same time - three rows were scheduled within 12 milliseconds - and the LLM and the decision model of that decision were both self-hosted on one CPU node. In single calls the retrieval node took 0.2 seconds, the LLM node 1.3 to 4.2 and the decision model node 1.7 to 4.1, a whole call 6.5 to 9 seconds, and six single calls one after another all returned `retrieval_status` *ok*, three hits, an envelope of 1969 characters and the answer of the LLM from the context. With three rows at once the service logged 0.2 to 2.2 seconds for the retrieval node, 5 to 6 seconds for the LLM node of the first row and more than 29 for the two others, and 24 to 25 seconds for the decision model node while the LLM container was busy; with the retrieved context the prompt of the LLM is about 900 characters instead of about 50. The same module with the same table, validated twice without any change, returned all three rows empty the first time and one row complete - its three nodes took 29.65 seconds in total - and two empty the second time; three direct calls at once all answered `timedOut`, after 30.8, 31.7 and 36.5 seconds. The lengths of the model's variables have no part in it (see *Changed*). What a site can do follows from these durations and was not measured as a remedy: fewer calls at once, a faster model or models that do not share a node, a shorter prompt - a smaller `k` or smaller chunks. No setting for the 30 seconds is named, because none was found: the configuration definition `sas.microanalyticservice.system` of that system holds no timeout property, and no way to change the time was tried. *Several rows at once* on the *RAG Ingestion and Retrieval* page has the durations side by side, and the *Troubleshooting Guide* the section for the symptom.

### Changed

- **The RAG Builder is part of the Prompt Builder.** One bundle (`LLM-Prompt-Builder/dist/index.html`), one job definition (`SAS-LLM-Prompt-Builder-UI`), one report (`Prompt Builder`) and one Options pane serve all three kinds. The names and paths of the Prompt Builder are unchanged, so existing Data-Driven Content URLs, bookmarks and saved `builder-options.json` files keep working, and every element of both builders keeps its id. *RAG Builder* stays the name of the RAG part of the Prompt Builder; there is no report, object, package, job definition or build of that name any more.
- **One list of options for the Prompt Builder, and two compute contexts with two names.** The Options pane shows the shared options first (SAS Viya host, repository, LLM project, SCR endpoint, deployment type, judge model, model card report, credential domain), then each switch with its own options. The repository, the SCR endpoint, the deployment type and the credential domain are set once and used by every kind. `computeContext` keeps meaning the context of the optimization job; the RAG Builder's ingestion context is the option `ragComputeContext` (**RAG: ingestion compute context**), because the stock context the optimization job runs in is known not to work for an ingestion. A blank value in a URL is read as the default, and the `id` parameter is no longer read.
- **`mdb package-export` exports the content root and keeps what the manifest lists.** Every other object of the export is dropped with its entry in the requested items of the package and its mention in the connectors of what stays - a generated artifact, a report somebody parked in the folder, a dependency from outside it -, and so is every authorization rule and share. Of the files a report brings along, those stay that are its children and that its manifest entry lists (see *Changed*, the pictures of a report). In every copy of the Prompt Builder report the option defaults are reset to the neutral values - no project ids, RAG and optimization off, TLS verification on -, the per-user states are removed and the exporting host becomes `your-sas-viya-host`. The result is compared with the checkout before it is written, as `mdb package-check` does it, and the file from before stays byte for byte when a difference, a host or an object on a path that never ships is left. `--folder` is now `--content-root`, `--exclude` is gone, an export of another folder than the content root needs `--out`, and `--allow-differences` - with `--out` only - writes the export of a test folder that holds no report. Measured on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12 on the test content root. Without the two reports in the folder the command ended with exit code 1 and wrote no file, with one line per report that it is in the manifest but not in the package. With `--allow-differences` it wrote 71 objects and left nine out: the five objects of the folder that the manifest does not list, and the four folders above the content root, each as `(not below the content root)`. The export of that folder brought no rule. The file held nothing outside the manifest, no host, no rule and no share. It held the user id of the exporter 215 times - in `createdBy` and `modifiedBy` of the package, of every object and inside every body - and the path of the exported folder in `contentSourceLocation` of every object; they are left as the export writes them, and the command says since which user ids the file names. With copies of the two reports of the test system in the folder the export went through without `--allow-differences`: nine option defaults reset, the host replaced by the placeholder in four places, 77 objects, four of them the pictures of the usage report. After a file, a custom step and a job definition had been changed by hand it ended with exit code 1, named them and wrote nothing. Not measured: an export of the content folder itself, and whether such an export brings rules.
- **`mdb builders-import` hands only the reports to the transfer service and brings everything else in place.** The import of 2026-10-12 (see *Added*) showed two things about the SAS Viya transfer service: a file that is in the target folder under the same name with another id is not replaced - the file of the package is written into no folder, its task ends `completedWithWarnings`, and the import job still says `completed` -, and a package whose root folder is not at the root of SAS Content lands in a new folder of that name at the root. The command therefore hands the transfer service only the reports, the files that are children of a report and the folders they sit in. Every file, custom step and job definition of the package is written by the requests `mdb content-deploy` sends, with the package as the source and no checkout needed for the content: an object that is at its path is updated under the id it has on the site, whatever id the package carries, and a missing one is created. One line per entry says `equal`, `updated`, `created` or `FAILED`, for an object that was there with its id: `updated step RAG/steps/RAG - Embed Chunks.step (templates; id <its id> kept)`. Nothing of the exporting system - an id, a link, a user - is written to the site with it. New options: `--content-root`, the folder for everything but the reports; `--no-reports`, with which the transfer service is not called at all; `--compute-context`; and `--no-options-capture`. A report is imported only into the folder the package was exported from, and only when that folder is at the root of SAS Content, which is how every release so far was installed; otherwise the reports are not imported, the command says why, and it ends with exit code 1 unless `--no-reports` says that this is meant. No mapping to another folder is guessed. The reports are imported first, so that on a first install the transfer service creates the folders as it did before. Not run against SAS Viya. Not measured either: what the transfer service does with the folders of the reduced package on a site whose folders of the same paths have other ids, and with a report of the same name under another id - the tasks of the job are read, so a refusal ends the run with exit code 1 and its line.
- **`mdb content-deploy` writes in place and stops at the first write that fails.** As first built for this release the command replaced a file that differed by deleting it and uploading the new copy, and went on with the next entry after a failure. A review of that code against a service that is made up found what this costs: with uploads that are refused, every file that differed was gone from the folder, and with an upload that got no answer the line did not say that the file was deleted. On 2026-10-12 a file the command replaced had a new id afterwards (see *Added*). A file that differs now gets its content replaced - `PUT /files/files/{id}/content` under the ETag of the file - and stays the file it was; nothing is deleted, and the repeat of an upload that is answered 409 is gone with the delete. A write that is refused or gets no answer ends the run: the entry is `FAILED`, every entry behind it is listed as `not attempted`, and the line `Stopped at the first write that failed: ...` says what was written before it and that nothing was removed. Where the update of a file is refused, the file is left as it is. An entry that fails before anything is sent - two objects of its name in the folder, a reference to an object elsewhere, an object that cannot be read - is reported and the run goes on, and so does a run with a folder that cannot be created: what belongs into that folder fails without a write. Either way the exit code is 1. Not run against SAS Viya: the update of the content of a file is written from the documentation of the Files service and has not been sent to one.
- **`mdb package-check` is strict for a release and lenient between two releases.** The package is exported from a system, so between two exports it holds the content of an earlier commit: every changelog entry, every fact sheet row and every fix to a program made it differ from the checkout, and the workflow and six tests of `mdb` would have failed for each of them until a maintainer exported again. `mdb package-check --release` is what the release procedure runs after the export and what the workflow runs for a version tag: every difference is an error. Without `--release` a difference in content against the checkout - a file, a custom step, a job definition, the release table, `release.json` - is a line that starts with `drift:` and does not fail. What a package must never be fails as before: an object the manifest does not list or lists once and the package holds twice, a rule, a share, a host, a per-user state, a report default that is not neutral, a missing entry, a path that never ships. With `--release` the option `--allow-missing` has no effect: a release cannot be tagged without the package. `CONTRIBUTING.md` says what a contributor sees. `release.json` names the commit the folder was deployed from, and for a release that commit has to be in the history of the checkout - a folder deployed from a commit of a branch that is squashed afterwards fails the gate of the tag. The workflow was not run with the change.
- **The content manifest marks the settings of a site, and lists the pictures of a report.** A parameter of a job definition with `siteSetting: true` - `_contextName` and `contentRoot` - holds a value of the site: a new job definition gets the default, and on a site that has the job definition a deploy and an import leave its value alone and do not count it as a difference; `--compute-context` writes a compute context into every job definition that has `_contextName`, and `--content-root`, when it is given, writes that folder into `contentRoot` of `Start Here` and `Load-Accelerator-Tables`. As first built, every deploy set a compute context a site had chosen for the Prompt Builder job back to `SAS Studio compute context`, and a deploy into another content root left the page checking the default folder. A report entry lists the files that are children of the report under `files:`, with the SHA-256 of each: the four pictures of the *LLM Usage Report*, as the package of earlier releases and the export of 2026-10-12 hold them. `mdb package-export` keeps those and no other file a report brings along, and `mdb package-check` compares names and hashes. Not run against SAS Viya.
- **`deploy-rag-content.ps1` / `.sh` call `mdb content-deploy --only RAG --to <the RAG content root>`.** They need the Model Definition Builder installed and sign in the way it does - a token, a password grant or the login of the SAS Viya CLI -, where they read the token of the CLI themselves before. The RAG content root they are given (`-ContentRoot` / `-r`) is the folder the runtime goes into, whatever its name: `--to` names the folder the entries below `RAG` of the manifest are deployed into. `-DryRun` / `-n` lists what would change. A site whose RAG content root is the folder `RAG` of the content folder needs neither script: the import brings the runtime there. Both scripts were checked for their syntax; neither ran against SAS Viya.
- **Files reach SAS Content with LF line ends.** `mdb content-deploy` uploads a repository file with LF line ends whatever the checkout holds - a Windows checkout has CRLF where the repository has LF -, so that the same file has the same hash in `release.json` from every checkout. The scripts uploaded the bytes of the checkout. Measured on 2026-10-12: read back from the test content root, every repository file had the bytes of the repository with LF line ends.
- **A refused password grant is asked again.** A call of `mdb` ended with `401 Client Error` at the sign-in on the test system on 2026-10-11 and went through when it was repeated; the sign-in service refused password grants that overlapped that day. `mdb` now asks up to three times, 2 and 5 seconds apart. A wrong password is refused each time, `MDB_LOGIN_ATTEMPTS=1` switches the repeat off, and a token is not asked again. On 2026-10-12 a refused grant was not asked again and the command ended with a traceback: see *Fixed*.
- **`mdb setup` writes one seed file for the Prompt Builder.** `llm-prompt-builder.json` now also names the embedding project (`embeddingProjectID`) and holds exactly the five values an administrator copies into the Options pane - repository, LLM project, embedding project, SCR endpoint, deployment type; the keys `name`, `id`, `width`, `type` and `API_KEYS`, which no option corresponds to, are gone. `mdb builders-import` fills in the same five by itself, and a failing lookup of the embedding project no longer costs the LLM values.
- **The release table files RAG Builder changes under the Prompt Builder.** The `component` column of `ACCELERATOR_RELEASES` no longer has the value `RAG Builder`: entries about it - past ones included - are `Prompt Builder` rows, which is what the changelog page of the report filters on - but for the one of 2.0.4 about the release table itself (see the next entry). `RAG runtime` stays a component of its own.
- **The release table files 14 entries of earlier releases under another component.** The value of `component` in `ACCELERATOR_RELEASES` changes for them when the table is reloaded, under a report imported earlier as well. No entry of a release before 2.0.0 is filed under `RAG runtime` any more: `rag_core`, the RAG steps and the vector stores came with 2.0.0, and five older entries were filed there for a word such as *retrieves* or *enrichment*. Two of them are `Model Definition Builder` rows now (1.8.3 *Live catalogs no longer lose the kind*, 1.6.0 *Richer model attributes on register*), one is `Definitions` (1.1.0, the open-source CPU embedding definitions), one is `General` (0.1.15, `Get-All-Prompts.sas`) and one `Prompt Builder` (1.7.0 *The manifested best prompt inherits its winning LLM's attributes*). An entry about the best prompt is a `Prompt Builder` entry, which moves five more there from `Model Definition Builder` or `General`: 1.5.0 *Save vs. manifest link placement*, three of 1.0.0 (the tags a manifest writes, the toasts after saving and manifesting, *Save Experiments* and *Manifest Best Prompt* as real buttons) and 0.1.8 (the line in the language file that explains the best prompt). And an entry that says it holds for both builders is a `Prompt Builder` entry for every part its release had: the two fixes of 2.0.0 to the live run displays (*A carriage return would have made every progress milestone unreadable*, *Long progress messages were being read as their first 132 characters*) were `RAG runtime` rows and are `Prompt Builder` rows for `Prompt` and for `RAG`. An entry whose headline is about the release table - it names the release table, `ACCELERATOR_RELEASES` or `mdb load-releases` - is a `Model Definition Builder` entry: `mdb` makes the table, and such an entry names the Prompt Builder, the RAG Builder or the RAG runtime as values of the column, not as what changed. 2.0.4 *A release table for the Builder objects: `mdb load-releases`* was a `RAG Builder` row and is the one entry with that value that does not become a `Prompt Builder` row; the entries of this release about the table are filed the same way and have an empty `builder_part`. And an entry that the wording files under `Model Definition Builder` is filed under `Definitions` when its headline names the definitions and nothing of `mdb`, although its text says what `mdb` generated or renders: 2.2.0 *Shipped definitions*, and the 1.1.0 entry named above. Beside `component` and the new `builder_part`, no column of an existing row changes. Not run in SAS Viya: the table was built as CSV from this changelog and compared entry by entry with the one the rules built before, and the tests of `mdb` cover the rules. See *The release table* in the Model Definition Builder guide. Counted on the table as CSV - built from the changelog of 2.2.0 with the `mdb` of 2.2.0, and from this changelog with the `mdb` of this release: of the 453 entries of the releases up to 2.2.0, 61 were filed under `Prompt Builder` before and 98 are now - the 29 that were `RAG Builder` rows and eight of these 14, of releases up to 2.0.0 -, and they are 113 rows of the table where they were 61, an entry being one row for each part it concerns. A page filtered on `Prompt Builder` therefore lists more than those eight. Upgrading from 2.2.x: nothing by hand - the import of step 2 reloads the table.
- **`LLMCONTAINERSSLVERIFY` switches TLS verification on as well as off.** The score code of a prompt template with the included LLM call and of a decision template manifested in the Prompt Builder read the variable in one direction only: `false`, `no` or `0` switched verification off. `true`, `yes` or `1` now switch it on, also for a model that was manifested with verification off, so the variable wins in both directions on the destination it is set on. Unset, or with any other word, the model does what was written into it when it was manifested. A model manifested with the option ticked behaves as before for every value of the variable; `LLMCONTAINERCABUNDLE` and `LLMCONTAINERTIMEOUT` are unchanged.
- **`RAGEMBED_SSLVERIFY` and `RAGSTORE_SSLVERIFY` of the retrieval model switch verification on as well as off.** `retrieve_context.py` verifies its two HTTPS calls - to the embedding container and, in a compute session, to the credentials service - and read the two variables in one direction only. `false`, `no` or `0` still switch verification off; `true`, `yes` or `1` now switch it on, and unset or with any other word the new constant `SSL_VERIFY` of the MANIFEST block decides, which is `True` in the template and written per model when the model is manifested. The connection to the vector store is not affected: its TLS stays the matter of `sslmode`. The template reaches a site with the transfer package (step 2 of the upgrade), and a model has it once it is manifested again (step 8). A model manifested from a template deployed before this release has no such constant and verifies whatever the option of the report says: the manifest then says that verification is still on and that the template has to be deployed again, and the model is not tagged.
- **The retrieval model prefers the endpoint it was manifested with to `SAS_SERVICES_URL`.** `retrieve_context.py` embeds the question at the first of these that is not blank: `scr_endpoint` in the options of the call, the environment variable `RAGEMBED_ENDPOINT`, the manifested `EMBED_ENDPOINT`, and `SAS_SERVICES_URL` + `/llm`. In 2.2.x `SAS_SERVICES_URL` came before the manifested value, so wherever that variable is set - a compute session sets it - the question was embedded at `<SAS Viya host>/llm` whatever the model had been manifested for. Where the model containers are served somewhere else, that is another container than the one that embedded the documents, or none. Without `SAS_SERVICES_URL` nothing changes. The order is part of the template: a model has it once the template of this release is in the content folder (step 2 of the upgrade) and the model has been manifested again (step 8).
- **The log collection script redacts secrets and reads the model pods only.** With `SAS_SCR_LOG_LEVEL_SCR_IO=TRACE`, which the log parser needs, a container logs the options of every request - with the provider key of every model that is called with one, as `API_KEY:<key>` - and the request headers, with the `Cookie` header of every call made from the browser. The script in *Logging & Monitoring* of the Administration Guide copied all of it into a file that SAS Studio users can open. It now pipes every line through `sed` before it is written and replaces the key and the values of the `Cookie`, `Authorization` and `X-CSRF-TOKEN` headers with `REDACTED`; the log parser reads a redacted log exactly like an unredacted one. It also selects the pods by the label the deployment templates give every model (`-l app`) instead of taking every pod in the namespace, where one unrelated service made up 97 % of the collected file on the test deployment. The guide gains a warning about the files collected so far, and says which lines the redaction expects: the three header expressions match a header at the start of a line, as `kubectl logs` prints it without `--timestamps` or `--prefix`. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11: one provider key and seven `Cookie` headers in the logs were `REDACTED` in the written file, and the number of lines stayed the same. The expressions for `Authorization` and `X-CSRF-TOKEN` were not exercised there, because no such header occurred.
- **Appending in the log parser writes the table again, wider, and keeps its other columns.** Appending called `table.append`, which takes rows into the columns the table has. It now writes the rows of the existing table and the rows of the log to one new table with a DATA step and puts that table in the place of the existing one, the way replace mode does: the 36 columns of the parser stand in front in their order, a column the existing table lacks is added and is missing in its rows, a column the parser does not know stays behind the others with its type and length and is missing in the new rows, and what the link wrote into existing rows stays. Text columns of the parser are VARCHAR; one that the existing table holds as CHAR is read under another name and handed to the VARCHAR column without its trailing blanks, so new rows are not cut to the old length. A column that is a number on one side and text on the other stops the DATA step, and the table is left as it is with the error `The new table could not be created, ...`. A table that was promoted is promoted again whatever the option of the run says; the saved copy is replaced when saving is on. The new table only takes the place of the existing one when the DATA step ended with a return code of at most 4 and wrote as many rows as the existing table and the log hold together - otherwise one ERROR line names the return code and the row counts, and the table stays as it is -, and it gets the label of the existing table. For this the program assigns the librefs `_llmtgt` and `_llmsrc` and clears them, keeps the column list of the table in `work._log_target_columns` for a moment, and reads the table through a libref - its name has to be one a libref can address (32 characters). Appending writes the whole table on every run, and other sessions do not see the table between the drop and the promote, as in replace mode. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11, with the program as it was that day, on a table of twelve columns and 174 rows that the parser had written before it had the further columns: appending the log of 1040 requests gave 1214 rows and 36 columns, promoted and saved again, the earlier rows with missing values in the new columns; into a copy with CHAR text columns no value was cut - the longest were those of a fresh parse -; a copy with two further columns kept them behind the 36; and a copy whose `temperature` was text gave `ERROR: Variable temperature has been defined as both character and numeric.` and `ERROR: The new table could not be created, ...`, and was unchanged. The DATA step ran in CAS. After the append the table had lost its label. The check of the return code and of the rows, and the label, were added after these runs and ran on 2026-10-12: the append gave 1214 rows and 36 columns again, with the label of the table, and a copy with a label of the site's own kept it; the copy whose `temperature` was text gave, behind the ERROR of the DATA step, the line with the return code 1012 and -1 rows written, was unchanged, and its job ended as failed.
- **`PROMPT_EXPERIMENTS` gains twelve columns behind the 49 it had.** `modelID`, `projectID` and `projectName` (the ids were only inside `modelLink` and `projectLink`), `kind` (`prompt` or `decision`), `expected` (the expected answers of a decision run, JSON), `options` (the options of the run as stored, with the value of an option whose name says that it holds a key or a password replaced by `REDACTED`, by the names the log parser goes by), `variables` (the variables of the run, JSON), `resolved_system_prompt` and `resolved_user_prompt` (the prompts as they were sent), `resolved_system_hash` and `resolved_user_hash` (SHA-256 of those texts with every run of blanks as one, taken before a text is cut to the length of its column) and `judge_reasoning` (at most 2,000 bytes). The 49 columns keep their names, their order and their types. A long text column - the prompts, the response, the description - is now as wide as its longest value and at most 32,767 bytes, where it was 32,767 for every row; `description` holds up to 1,024 bytes, where it was cut at 256. `max_tokens` also takes the value of the option `max_length` when a run has no `max_tokens`. `unique_id` is still the row number and no key. The table was written on the test system on 2026-10-11 with its 61 columns and 318 rows; the 38 columns the *LLM Usage Report* reads are among them under their names, and the report was not opened on the table.
- **`Get-All-Prompts.sas` reads SAS Model Manager in PROC PYTHON.** The requests are made with the Python package `requests`, the token of the session and the CA bundle of the compute server, as the optimization job makes them; PROC HTTP and the JSON libname engine no longer read the service. The Python step hands each table to SAS as a JSON file with a map in the WORK folder. Of the WORK tables, `_gap_all_data` stays, and `_gap_all_optimizations`, `_gap_all_rag_setups`, `_gap_all_rag_runs` and `_gap_all_catalog` are new; `_gap_all_projects`, `_gap_all_models` and `_gap_all_experiments` are gone. Run on the test system on 2026-10-11: the JSON libname engine read the files and their maps as they are written.
- **The *LLM - Get All Prompts* step (version 2.2) writes the five tables.** Its output table names the table of the experiments and the CAS library, as before; the section *Further output tables* has a field for each of the four other tables, and *Where the models are found* the two tags and the three projects. A table or tag field that is left empty takes the default, a project field that is left empty leaves that kind out of the catalog. The step starts a CAS session only when the session the option `SESSREF` names is not connected, and ends the one it started. Version 2.0 of the step ran on the test system on 2026-10-11, as the code SAS Studio generated for a flow, and wrote the five tables with the content the program wrote. Version 2.1 - the table names it refuses, the further source of `RAG_RUNS`, the further columns and roles of the catalog - ran there on 2026-10-12 the same way and wrote the five tables of the program of that day, with the same three WARNINGs. What version 2.2 adds - one ERROR line without an ABORT for an output library that is no CAS library, the condition code 1012 for a step that wrote an ERROR line and left an output table unwritten, the function its messages go through, the counter for an id that a tracker holds twice - ran there later that day (see *The three programs and the ingestion job ran once more on SAS Viya* under *Added*). Version 2.3 of the step - the counter that passes over the id of another entry, see *Fixed* - is not run in SAS.
- **German: the prompt part of the Prompt Builder says "manifestieren" too.** Eleven German texts of the prompt template part said *festschreiben* for what the decision and RAG parts already called *manifestieren*: the button (*Besten Prompt manifestieren*), the heading and the description of the manifest card, the label and the info text of the included LLM call, the label of the default outputs, the toast (*Der beste Prompt wurde manifestiert.*), the message for a manifest without an output, the two *Nicht manifestiert:* messages for a saved run with a refused variable or question name, and the explanation under the prompts on *Build & Test*. No key and no English text changed.
- **The Prompt Builder and `RAG - Register Setup` register the retrieval model of a RAG setup with the same lengths, every variable with one.** The RAG Builder wrote the two context outputs of the retrieval model, `context_dg` and `context_envelope`, without a length, for which SAS Model Manager stored 99 and SAS Intelligent Decisioning declared `in_out varchar(100)` in the code it generates for a decision; *RAG - Register Setup* registered the two outputs with 32000 and its inputs shorter than the Builder's (4096, 1024, 16 and 2048). Both now write `question` 32000, `k` 8, `filter_json` and `options` 4000, `retrieval_mode` 32, `context_dg` and `context_envelope` 32000, `retrieval_status` 512 and `run_time` 8 - the Builder with every save and every manifest, as before - and a test compares the two lists entry by entry, so that a model is the same model whichever of the two wrote its variables last. The lengths describe the model and repair nothing. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, as a control: with both context outputs set back to 99 on the model and the decision published again, single calls in SAS Micro Analytic Service returned HTTP status 201, `retrieval_status` *ok* and `context_envelope` complete with its 1969 characters, and the test of SAS Intelligent Decisioning had returned the longer values complete with the length 99 as well - a value longer than its declared length neither failed the execution nor was it cut. The empty outputs that a publishing validation returned for such a decision were executions the service had ended after 30 seconds (see *Added*); the validation that returned every row after the lengths had been raised to 32000 by hand was a run that finished within that time. Measured the same day with the Prompt Builder page of this release, the bundle that ships: **Manifest setup** wrote the nine lengths, 32000 for both context outputs. On the same page, **Launch ingestion** of a setup on the runtime of this release ended as completed, with run ids of the form `run-<epoch>-<ten digits>` and the run history recorded, and three runs of one setup showed 6 documents new, then 6 changed, then 6 unchanged and 1 new. Measured earlier that day with the page as it was before its German texts were reworded (the entry above): the same nine lengths, and the code SAS Intelligent Decisioning then generated for a decision declared `varchar(32000)` for both context outputs. A model that *RAG - Register Setup* registered held the same nine lengths in SAS Model Manager. Not measured: a model that *RAG - Register Setup* registered with these lengths inside a decision, outputs longer than 32000 characters, SingleStore, and a value longer than its declared length for a model of another kind. Upgrading from 2.2.x: step 2 brings the page and step 8 manifests each RAG setup again, which is what writes the lengths; nothing else has to be done for them. The Administration Guide has the table under *The lengths of the variables* on the *RAG Ingestion and Retrieval* page.
- **A failed run of the RAG ingestion job ends as a failed job.** `Ingest-Documents.sas` never raises a SAS error, so it ended normally whatever happened and SAS Job Execution reported every run as completed; the outcome was in the log only. The program now sets `SYSCC` as its last statement when the run failed - the statement a job that *RAG - Register Setup* generates from a flow ends with, which SAS Job Execution reported as `failed` when that job was measured (2026-10-11, SAS Viya 2026.09). A schedule or a monitor that reads the state of the job now sees a failed ingestion as one. The program still runs to its last statement and nothing aborts. Every run that failed ends this way, whatever the reason: a saved ledger that cannot be loaded, the lock of another run, a store that cannot be reached. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 for `Ingest-Documents.sas`, run in SAS Job Execution in the *SAS Studio compute context*, with pgvector: the nine runs that failed - three at a configuration that had drifted from the ledger, five at a fresh lock that was not theirs (two at the lock of a flow that was running, three at a lock row appended by hand, one of them a row of the earlier kind without a run id), one at a `rag_core` folder that does not exist - ended as `failed` after 16.5 to 20 seconds, and the eight runs that went through ended as `completed`. The record of a failed job holds the error code 5713 of SAS Job Execution, *A procedure in the SAS program provided for the job completed execution with at least one error.*, and inside it an error with the code 1012 and the text of the program's ERROR line. That line is the last message of the program and not the last line of the log: the timing lines of its DATA step and the echoed source of the last statement follow it. Not run: a saved ledger that cannot be loaded (see the entry about a saved ledger under *Fixed*) and a store that cannot be reached. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 8.
- **The RAG ingestion job publishes the run history to CAS.** `Ingest-Documents.sas` - the ingestion a setup is started with from its page, and the ingestion job of a setup - writes `<prefix>_RUNS` and `<prefix>_DOC_EVENTS` beside the ledger, promoted and saved, as *RAG - Load Vector Store* does: every run the vector store holds for the collection (the newest 500) and their document events (the newest 5,000), with the columns, types and labels of the step. `<prefix>` is the ledger table without its ending `_LEDGER`. The job recorded its runs in the vector store only: on the test system on 2026-10-11 and on 2026-10-12 a setup that had been ingested by the job had its `<prefix>_LEDGER` in memory and no `<prefix>_RUNS`, so the option text *publishes the first two to CAS for reporting* did not hold for it, and `Get-All-Prompts.sas` had only the newest run of such a setup, from its ledger. A run that finds nothing to do publishes too, so one run of an existing setup brings the runs the store holds into CAS. A history that cannot be read or published is a WARNING in the job log and not a failed run; with the history switched off the job touches neither table, and it leaves a table the store holds no rows for as it is, where the step drops the table and deletes its saved file. `rag_core/history.py` has the rows as the step stages them for a caller without SWAT (`cas_types`, `cas_values`, `write_cas_file`). `Get-All-Prompts.sas` reads the table of the job as it reads one of the step, with `run_source` = `run table`, and its ledger only where the run table is not in memory, so a run that both name is one row of `RAG_RUNS`. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-12 with six runs of the job, in a content folder, a table prefix and a collection of their own, each run a SAS Job Execution job that ended as completed. A first run of three documents published `<prefix>_RUNS` with one row and 35 columns and `<prefix>_DOC_EVENTS` with four rows and ten columns, promoted and saved; set against a run table the step had published, no column differed in name, type or label, and two differed in length. A run that found nothing to do made it two rows and five events, and a run with one changed document three and seven. With the history switched off the log had no line about it and both tables and their files stayed as they were. After both tables were dropped and their files deleted, one run published four rows - every run the store held; the run with the history switched off is not among them. With the `rag_core` of before this change the job wrote the milestone *rag history: not read for CAS (ImportError: …) - the ingestion itself is unaffected* and the WARNING, and left both tables. The pull then read the runs from the run table, and the link found the document embeddings of the runs (see *The three programs and the ingestion job ran once more on SAS Viya* under *Added*). Changed after those runs and not run in SAS: how the reason of a history that could not be read reaches SAS (see *Fixed*). The tests run the Python of the job against a stand-in for the store, compare the file it writes with what the step stages and hold the SAS statements of the upload as text, and they follow the two macros of `Get-All-Prompts.sas` that stack the run tables with a stand-in. Upgrading from 2.2.x: step 8.
- **`Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` replaces a table only with a sheet that was read.** Each sheet is read and loaded beside its table first; a file that is not in the folder, a file without rows and a sheet CAS did not hold completely leave the table as it is, with a WARNING, and the other sheets are loaded. The table is then dropped and loaded with the statements the program had, so a failure of CAS in that second load can still cost the table. The program switches the option `SYNTAXCHECK` off while it runs, so that an error for one sheet stops no sheet behind it in a batch session. Run on 2026-10-12 through the job `Load-Accelerator-Tables`, with the three table names replaced by test names - fifteen lines of the program - and nothing else changed: the three sheets were loaded with 30, 16 and 5 rows, promoted and saved, and with one file missing from the folder that sheet gave its WARNING and the two others were loaded. The program with its own table names was not run: it replaces the fact sheets of a system.
- **The steps *LLM - Log Parser* and *LLM - Get All Prompts* are version 2.5 and 2.3.** Both were built from their programs again and no control changed. Version 2.5 of the parser differs from 2.4 in the text of its *About* tab and in one comment; version 2.3 of the pull has the counter that passes over the id of another entry (see *Fixed*). The settings that are taken only where they are not set are in the two code files and not in the steps, which get every setting from a control. Both versions were registered in a test content root on 2026-10-12 by `mdb content-deploy`, and what the service held equalled the two files in every part that is compared; neither version has run as a step.
- **Start Here: a session that is refused everything gets one line.** Where SAS Viya answers every request of the session with 401 or 403, the page has one line at its top in place of those lines: that every request was refused, and that the page is opened with `&_contextName=<a compute context that runs as you>` or with that context in the parameter of the job definition. It says that the session runs under a service account, and not as you, where the user of the SAS session (`SYSUSERID`) is another one than the user SAS Job Execution started the session for (`SYS_COMPUTE_SESSION_OWNER`); the two are compared and neither is written to the page or the log. In such a session that only some services refuse, each refused line advises to open the page as yourself. Not run in SAS; that `SYSUSERID` is the service account in such a session was not printed by the job of 2026-10-12.
- **Load-Accelerator-Tables says when its session is another user's.** One NOTE: the SAS session does not run as the user who started the job, a file that is reported as not found may be one that account may not read, and the parameter `_contextName` names a compute context that runs as you. The state of the job is decided as before. Not run in SAS.
- **Custom step *LLM - Create LLM Call* 1.1.** With *Save the code to a filename?* ticked and no path - the values the step comes with - the code is written to a temporary file and the fileref `_slo_LLM` stays assigned, as the label of the path says; the program of version 1.0 held an ERROR line and an ABORT statement for that. With a path nothing changes. Read from the program and not run in SAS. Not changed: the step is expected to fail in PROC TRANSPOSE for a model whose `options.json` holds a `values` list, as three LLM definitions of the repository do.

### Fixed

- **A decision template manifested in the Prompt Builder can be scored.** 2.2.0 wrote the lines that return each question's answer at the wrong indentation, so the score code of every manifested decision template stopped with `SyntaxError: expected 'except' or 'finally' block` - the model registered, and nothing could run it, in SAS Intelligent Decisioning or anywhere else. The code is valid Python now, and the state template is escaped for the string it is placed in (backslashes, double quotes, braces); the manifest of a prompt template uses the same escaping. One visible consequence: a backslash typed in a prompt or a state stays a backslash in the manifested model, which is what the experiment sent - before, a typed `\n` became a line break. The designer also refuses, as an item to fix, a question name the score code cannot use: a Python keyword, a name the code uses itself, the name of a variable of the state, the `_confidence` output of another question, and a name longer than 21 characters (it was 32; the model also returns `<name>_confidence`, and a SAS name ends at 32). Templates manifested with 2.2.0 have to be manifested again.
- **A decision template stays in the decision view of the Prompt Builder.** SAS Model Manager drops the tags that are sent when a model is created, and the Prompt Builder told the kinds apart by a tag: after a reload a new decision template was listed among the LLM prompts and missing from the decision view. The kind is now read from the model's `algorithm` (`Decision-Template`), which Model Manager keeps, with the tag as a second sign; templates made with 2.2.0 carry the algorithm and are recognised without any change. A project's prompt-tests are listed with one request instead of two, and the tags are written after creation where the service left them out.
- **The optimization switch of the Prompt Builder shows what is stored.** **Enable prompt optimization (DSPy)** showed *Disabled* in the Options pane whatever the report stored, because Visual Analytics does not restore a dropdown whose keys are the words `true` and `false`: after every reload of the object it shows the first entry. The page itself followed the stored value, so the Optimize step was there while the pane said *Disabled*. The switch is a checkbox now, like the two new ones, and an existing report keeps its setting - a stored `true` shows as ticked. `mdb options-save` and `mdb options-restore` read and write the two words as before.
- **Prompt-tests made in the Prompt Builder record their modeler.** A misspelt field name meant that every prompt-test was created without one; new prompt-tests carry the signed-in user as modeler in SAS Model Manager. Existing ones are not changed.
- **An LLM run in the Prompt Builder keeps the results of the models that answered.** A model that failed ended the run at that model, and the answers of the models after it were discarded. Each failed model now has its own line with a hint - registered but no container answers at the endpoint (404 / 503), the endpoint cannot be reached, or the hint for the provider's status as before - a message says how many of the selected models did not answer, and the cost and the *fastest* and *fewest tokens* marks are worked out over the answers only. A run in which no model answered is taken back instead of being listed empty, also when the tracker was drawn again while its calls were out. Decision runs behave the same and name the failed models beside the skipped ones.
- **A RAG setup keeps its vector database in the Prompt Builder when the deployment no longer offers it.** A stored setup on a database that the Options pane withholds opened with an empty *Vector database* field, as if it had no store. It now opens with its database selected and marked *not offered for new setups*, and can still be read, saved, manifested, launched and tested; the embedding model and the caslib are kept the same way. A new setup starts with no vector database selected instead of the first in the list.
- **The Setup card of the Prompt Builder reads in the right order.** The question *What are you building?* was set larger than the title of the card it sits in and is now a smaller heading one level below that title, and the row of buttons under the pickers keeps a gap between its lines when it wraps on a narrow report object. Three kind cards fit in one row at 1024 px, in English and in German.
- **The RAG Builder part of the Prompt Builder survives a failed project listing.** When the RAG projects could not be listed, the whole builder failed to load; it now shows the reason with a **Try again** button and keeps the rest of the page. The note shown when no embedding model can be listed no longer says that a model name has to be typed - the field is a list, and without a listed model no setup can be saved.
- **The note about decisions that still use a prompt template, a decision template or a RAG setup appears on the first delete in the Prompt Builder.** Before **Delete Prompt**, **Delete Project**, **Delete setup** and **Delete project** ask for confirmation, the page asks SAS Viya which SAS Intelligent Decisioning decisions reference what is about to be deleted and names them in the dialog. On the first use of that service in a browser session the request was answered with HTTP 449 instead of a result, and the dialog said *Decision usage could not be verified* - or, for a RAG setup, *could not be checked* - for something a decision does use. The page now establishes the session with the service and sends the request again, once, for every SAS Viya service it calls; the *could not be verified* note is left for a service that really does not answer. The note is the only thing between the click and a broken decision, measured on 2026-10-11: SAS Model Manager does not refuse to delete a prompt or a RAG setup that a decision still uses (the delete requests were answered 204). The decision stays, and in SAS Intelligent Decisioning its model nodes then carry an error badge and **Validate** fails with *NOT_FOUND* for each deleted model. The User Guide says so where the dialogs are described.
- **An ingestion run of a RAG setup in the Prompt Builder is shown with the result it had.** The run panel took the word *failed* anywhere in the milestones as a failed run, and every step reports `0 failed` on a clean run - so a run that ingested everything was shown red with *The ingestion run did not succeed*. A run is now failed when the job did not complete or reports `Ingestion failed` with its reason. A run that completed is shown as completed, and one in which documents failed as completed with a warning that gives their number: the other documents are in the collection, and the ledger names each failed document with its error.
- **A RAG setup embeds its documents and its questions at one endpoint.** A question embedded at another container than the documents searches the collection with the wrong vectors, and three things were given the endpoint separately. The retrieval model that **Manifest setup** registers was written with `<SAS Viya host>/llm` whatever the **SCR endpoint** option of the Prompt Builder said. The Embed step of the generated SAS Studio flow got a blank endpoint whatever the option said, and embedded at the host of its compute session. And with a blank option the ingestion job worked out the host in its compute session, while the model carried the host of the page. The endpoint is now resolved once - the **SCR endpoint** option, and `<SAS Viya host>/llm` where it is blank, the host being the **SAS Viya host** option or the address the page is opened at - and the same value goes to the ingestion job, **Test retrieval**, the Embed step of the flow and the retrieval model. With a blank option that address has to be reachable from the compute session of the ingestion and from the publishing destination; where the page is opened under a name that is not, such as a port-forward or an alias of a reverse proxy, set the option: the ingestion otherwise calls an address it cannot reach, where it used to work out the host itself. The value is written when the setup is manifested, and the retrieval model needs the template of this release to prefer it in a compute session (see *Changed*): manifest every setup again, as step 8 of the upgrade says. A job definition and a flow generated before keep their old value until then.
- **Variable names of a prompt are checked like question names in the Prompt Builder.** Every variable of a prompt becomes an input of the manifested score code, and every output variable an output. A name that code cannot use - a Python keyword, or a name the code uses itself, such as `options`, `llm`, `endpoint` or `json` - was accepted and produced a model that could not be scored. *Define Variables* and the output variables now mark such a name as invalid and say why, as the question designer does for a question name, and a saved run that still has one is not manifested: rename the variable, run the experiment again and manifest from that run.
- **A prompt manifested for the Call LLM node in the Prompt Builder sends valid JSON.** The score code builds `llmBody` with `json.dumps`, as the variant with the included LLM call does: a backslash or a tab in a prompt or in an input value no longer breaks the body, and an apostrophe stays an apostrophe - 2.2.x turned it into a double quote, so `the customer's complaint` reached the LLM as `the customer"s complaint`. Prompts manifested with 2.2.x keep the old body until they are manifested again.
- **"Judge this run" in the Prompt Builder can be used right after a run.** After an experiment the button of the new run stayed disabled until the prompt-test was loaded again, because the tracker was drawn while the run still counted as running. It is enabled as soon as the run has ended. 2.2.0 had the same defect. A judgment that came back while a run was out left that run drawn without its answers; it is drawn with them when it ends. A run whose judgment is out keeps its button disabled, with the spinner, whatever draws the tracker again.
- **A manifest of a RAG setup that fails half way says so beside the button.** **Manifest setup** saves the setup and then writes the ingestion job, the SAS Studio flow and the retrieval model. When one of the later parts failed, the reason appeared at the top of the page while the green note *RAG setup saved* stayed beside the button, which read as a manifest that went through. The failure is now reported beside the button, where the result of the click is read: which stage stopped the manifest, the reason it gave, and what had been written by then. A save that fails says so beside **Save setup** as well.
- **A manifest whose score code was not stored is a manifest that failed, in the Prompt Builder.** SAS Model Manager answers the upload of a score code with a status, and all three manifests went on whatever it said: a toast said that the model was manifested, the tags were written, and for a RAG setup a model version was recorded - for a model that still held its old score code, or none. *Finalize* now stops there for a prompt template and for a decision template: no tags are written, no toast is shown, and a line in the manifest box says that the score code could not be stored, that the model keeps the score code and the tags it had, and that its input and output variables may already be those of this run. **Manifest setup** stops at the retrieval model with `SAS Model Manager did not store retrieve_context.py (HTTP <status>)` and records no model version.
- **A manifest in the Prompt Builder ends when a request fails outright.** A score code that SAS Model Manager refuses comes back as a status, and the manifest reports it, as the entry above says. A request that gets no answer, or an error page in place of an answer from SAS Model Manager - the 502 of an ingress - is another case: *Finalize* stopped where it stood for a prompt template and for a decision template, the button stayed disabled on *Saving...*, and nothing was said. The button is usable again now on every path. Where such a request stops the manifest, no toast says that the model was manifested, and a red line in the manifest box says where it stopped. *Not manifested: a request to SAS Model Manager failed before the score code was stored* - the model keeps the score code and the tags it had, and its input and output variables may already be those of this run. *The manifest was not completed: the score code of this run is stored, but a request to SAS Model Manager failed after that* - `requirements.json` and the attributes of the model may be missing or still those of an earlier manifest. In both cases manifest again, and if that fails too, check the connection to SAS Viya and the permissions on the model. Two requests after the score code do not stop the manifest, because the model holds its score code by then, and these lines are not for them. A tag update that fails, refused or outright, is named by its own line in the box while the manifest goes on, and the toast is shown when it reaches its end. The last step, which copies the attributes of the LLM or of the decision model onto the manifested model, is best effort: when it fails the toast is shown and the page says nothing. The upload of the score code itself and the save before the manifest have entries of their own below. Both manifests also write the tags of the model directly after the score code is stored, before `requirements.json` and the attributes, so `TLS-Verification-Off` matches the stored score code when a later request fails. **Manifest setup** of a RAG setup already ended every stage with a message. Both lines are there in German as well.
- **A score code whose upload gets no answer is reported as not known, in the Prompt Builder.** When the upload of the score code fails outright - the connection drops, or an ingress answers with its error page in place of SAS Model Manager - the service may have stored the code before the answer was lost, and the page cannot tell. For a prompt template and a decision template the manifest box said *Not manifested: the score code could not be stored* and *The model keeps the score code and the tags it had* all the same, which is only certain when SAS Model Manager answered with a refusal. The line for an upload without an answer now reads *The manifest was not completed, and it is not known whether the score code of this run is stored*, and goes on: the tags of the model were not updated, so `TLS-Verification-Off` may not say what the stored score code does, and manifest again. **Manifest setup** of a RAG setup gave the error text of the browser as its reason, under *The setup is not manifested*; it now stops at the retrieval model with *It is not known whether SAS Model Manager stored retrieve_context.py*, says the same about the tag, and its first line says that the manifest was not completed. The page reads nothing back to find out, so until a manifest completes, the tag of such a model may not match the score code it holds. A refusal with an answer keeps its wording. The three texts are there in German as well.
- **Save Experiments in the Prompt Builder ends when a request fails outright.** The save had the defect the manifest had: a request that got no answer, or an error page in place of one, left **Save Experiments** disabled on *Saving...* for good, and nothing was said. **Manifest Best Prompt** saves the experiments first, so the same failure there ended the click without a word, and a save that SAS Model Manager refused was followed by the manifest all the same. Now the button is usable again on every path, no toast says that the experiments were saved, and a red line says that the save did not finish - beside **Save Experiments**, or in the manifest box when the click was on **Manifest Best Prompt**. The line says what is not known: the stored experiments are removed before the new ones are written, so the prompt-test may hold the earlier experiments, these, or none. The runs are still on the page; save again. A manifest does not go on after a save that failed, refused or outright: the box adds *Not manifested: a manifest saves the experiments first, and SAS Model Manager did not confirm that save*, and the model keeps the score code, the tags and the variables it had. **Run optimization** and **Compare targets** save first as well; after a save that failed they now stop with *Could not launch the optimization job* and the reason, where a refused save was followed by a job that read the experiments saved before. The three texts are there in German as well.
- **A double click on Manifest Best Prompt in the Prompt Builder runs one manifest.** The button was disabled only after the experiments had been saved, so a second click in that moment started a second manifest beside the first: the experiments were saved twice and two score codes were uploaded, and a failure line of one run could stand under the other. The click now takes the button at once and gives it back when the run has ended, for a prompt template and for a decision template.
- **The create dialogs of the Prompt Builder show the whole name.** In the dialogs that create a project, a prompt-test or a RAG setup the label and the input field shared one line, and the field was too narrow to show a name of ordinary length while it was typed. The field now has the room for it.
- **Delete project and Delete setup of the RAG Builder act on what was selected at the click.** Both first ask SAS Viya which decisions use what is about to be deleted, and that takes a moment. A change of the selected project or setup in that moment made the dialog name one object while the delete removed another - at worst the setups of the project just left, under the name of an empty one. The project and the setup are now fixed at the click: **Delete project** lists the setups of that project again from SAS Model Manager and deletes nothing when the listing fails, **Delete setup** opens no dialog when another setup was chosen in the meantime, and a change of project empties the setup list at once.
- **The prompt flow of the Prompt Builder cannot be used while the RAG Builder loads.** The first choice of the RAG setup card loads the RAG Builder, and the prompt steps stayed usable while it did. A dialog opened in that moment - *Create a new Project*, for one - was hidden together with the prompt steps when the RAG steps appeared, and its backdrop stayed over the page, so nothing could be clicked until a reload. The prompt flow does not react while the RAG Builder is loaded for the first time, and reacts again when the load ends or fails.
- **Arrow keys on the kind cards of the Prompt Builder move without choosing.** The cards under *What are you building?* are a radio group, and an arrow key on one chose the next kind at once: the selected project was reset, runs that were not saved were dropped without a question, and a second key press could open the RAG Builder. An arrow key now only moves the focus; Space chooses the card that has it.
- **A run in flight belongs to its prompt-test in the Prompt Builder.** Opening another prompt-test or project while an experiment was still running let the late answers land in the tracker that was open by then: the page stopped with an error and **Run Experiments** stayed on *Running Experiments...*, or, when every model failed, a saved run of the other prompt-test was taken out of its tracker and missing after the next manifest. Answers for a prompt-test that is no longer open are now dropped, and **Run Experiments** stays disabled until the calls of the run have ended.
- **The Prompt Builder draws the prompt-test its picker names.** Opening a prompt-test takes several requests to SAS Model Manager, and nothing looked at the picker again after them. With another prompt-test or the placeholder chosen while the first was still loading, its runs, its link and its **Delete Prompt** button were drawn under the other name, and **Save** wrote those runs to the prompt-test the picker named. The answer to an earlier choice is now dropped - for a prompt-test, for the prompt-tests of a project and for the documentation of a prompt-test - and a change of kind and both deletes drop what is still on its way. The documentation section is emptied and switched off wherever the selection is dropped: another project, another kind, a deleted prompt-test or project. Before, **Save documentation** went on writing to the prompt-test that had been open. Choosing the placeholder entry of the project list or of the prompt-test list no longer sends its text to SAS Model Manager as an id.
- **Create Prompt and Create Project in the Prompt Builder report a refused creation.** When SAS Model Manager refused to create a prompt-test - a name that exists already, no permission to write - the dialog closed, an entry named `undefined` was added to the list and selected, and every later request went to a model with that id. The dialog now stays open, a message says that nothing was created and what to check, and nothing is added to the list; the dialog for a new RAG setup reports a refusal in the same way. The message stands inside the dialog, in all four create dialogs: the RAG Builder wrote a missing name and a refusal to the banner at the top of the page, behind the backdrop of the dialog, so the click seemed to do nothing. **Create** is disabled while a creation is out - a double click created two RAG projects, or a setup and a refusal for its name.
- **The create dialogs of the Prompt Builder send nothing without a name.** *Create a new Project* with the name field empty sent the request all the same, and the dialog then said that the project was not created because a project of this name may already exist, or you may not be allowed to write there - neither of which was the matter. All four create dialogs - *Create a new Project*, *Create a new Prompt*, and *Create project* and *Create setup* of the RAG Builder - now refuse a name that is empty or blanks only themselves: no request is sent, the dialog says *A project name is required.* (or *A prompt name is required.*, *A setup name is required.*), the name field is marked and has the cursor, and the button stays usable. The two new texts are there in German as well.
- **A name that shows nothing is no name in the create dialogs of the Prompt Builder.** A name made of a zero-width space, or of other characters that are not displayed, with or without blanks, passed the check for an empty name and was sent to SAS Model Manager. All four create dialogs now treat such a name as empty: the characters Unicode sets apart as not displayed - the zero-width space, the zero-width joiner and non-joiner, the word joiner, the byte order mark, the soft hyphen, the direction marks and the others of the property `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point` - count like white space. A name can look empty without any of them: the braille pattern blank (U+2800) is drawn as nothing, is neither white space nor in that property, and a name made of it was still sent. The dialogs now ask whether a name has a visible character at all. Not visible are white space, the characters of `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point` - the Hangul fillers, the combining grapheme joiner, the Mongolian vowel separator, the Arabic letter mark, the tag characters and the variation selectors are among them -, the control characters, and three characters whose shape is defined as empty: the braille pattern blank, the musical null notehead (U+1D159) and the Khitan small script filler (U+16FE4). Every other character makes a name - a letter of any script, a digit, punctuation, a symbol, an emoji, also one put together with zero-width joiners or a variation selector. A name that has a visible character is sent as before: as typed in the two dialogs for a project and a prompt, with the blanks at its ends removed in the two RAG dialogs.
- **The create dialogs of the Prompt Builder create what stood in them at the click.** *Create a new Project* asks SAS Model Manager for the repository before it creates the project, and read the name field again after that answer: a name cleared in that moment was sent as an empty one, past the check of the dialog. The dialog now hands the name and the description of the click to the creation, in all four create dialogs, and the two fields cannot be edited while a creation is out; they can again when it has ended.
- **A create dialog of the Prompt Builder says so when a request on the way to a creation fails.** *Create a new Project* asks SAS Model Manager for the repository before it creates the project.
